## v4.98.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 89: **saved-views bookmark list on `/observability/sessions`.** Root cause: Step 88 shipped one-shot copy-URL but there was no way to re-open a labeled view (e.g. "Failed worker runs") without re-typing filters or trusting a raw browser bookmark that silently rots when the search schema evolves. Fix: added `src/lib/observability/savedViews.ts` (SSR-safe localStorage wrapper under `hmi.observability.sessions.savedViews.v1`, cap 20 views, 64-char names, JSON-parse-hardened `safeRead` that returns `[]` on corrupt input with a `console.warn` breadcrumb, `safeWrite` that catches quota errors the same way) exposing `listSavedViews`, `addSavedView` (returns `{ok, reason}` for duplicate-name / empty / over-limit rejections), `removeSavedView`. Wired into `src/routes/observability.sessions.tsx`: lazy `useEffect` seed (avoids SSR mismatch), `handleSaveView` snapshots the currently-active search post-`stripSearchParams` so persisted views stay canonical, `handleApplyView` calls `navigate({ search: view.search as SearchState, replace: true })` so `validateSearch` + `fallback()` auto-repair views saved before a future schema change, and a new saved-views strip UI (bookmark chips with inline delete + right-aligned "Save current" form) surfaces success/failure via sonner with per-action context. Verified: `bunx tsgo --noEmit` clean. Version bump: v4.97.0 -> v4.98.0.

## v4.97.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 88: **shareable view URL + `retainSearchParams(['debug'])` on `/observability/sessions`.** Root cause: the route had `stripSearchParams` but no `retainSearchParams`, so any operator-added `?debug=<tag>` troubleshooting flag got wiped the first time a filter/sort/limit/auto-refresh changed (because `patch()` merges only known SearchState fields), and there was zero UI affordance to hand off the current filtered/sorted view. Fix: added `debug: fallback(z.string(), "").default("")` to the search schema and layered `retainSearchParams(["debug"])` before `stripSearchParams(SEARCH_DEFAULTS)` under `search.middlewares` so `debug` survives every navigation but empty values still stay out of shared URLs; added a "Copy view URL" button in the header that reads `window.location.href` (post-middleware, so it matches exactly what a bookmarker sees) and surfaces success/failure via sonner with the URL in the toast description and a `console.warn` breadcrumb on clipboard failure. Verified: `bunx tsgo --noEmit` clean. Version bump: v4.96.0 -> v4.97.0.

## v4.96.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 87: **harden `validateSearch` on `/observability/sessions` to the `tanstack-search-params` guideline.** Root cause (one sentence): Step 86 shipped a `SearchSchema` built from closed `z.enum([...])` and `z.number().int().min(1).max(500)` and passed the raw result through `SearchSchema.parse(...)` without `fallback()`, which the guideline explicitly forbids because any out-of-range URL value (`?limit=9999`, `?cli=bogus`, `?sort=whatever`) throws inside `validateSearch` and takes the whole route down instead of degrading gracefully. Fix: switched to `zodValidator(searchSchema)` from `@tanstack/zod-adapter` (`^1.167.0`, added this turn) with adapter `fallback()` on every field over open primitives (`z.string`, `z.number`, `z.boolean`); bad URLs now silently fall back to `SEARCH_DEFAULTS`. Added `stripSearchParams(SEARCH_DEFAULTS)` under `search.middlewares` so defaulted fields never appear in the URL, retiring the six manual `delete merged.field` lines that also triggered TS2790 once fields became non-optional. Component now whitelists/clamps the loose primitive values (`cli` and `status` against literal-union guards, `limit` clamped 1..500 with `Math.trunc`, `sort`/`dir` against `SORT_VALUES`/`DIR_VALUES` readonly arrays) before use, so BE requests and dropdown values stay coherent even when the URL carries junk. `patch()` shrinks to a plain merge + `navigate({ replace: true })` because the middleware handles default stripping. Verified: `bunx tsgo --noEmit` clean end-to-end. Version bump: v4.95.0 -> v4.96.0.

Plan 90 Step 86: **URL-persisted view state on `/observability/sessions`.** Root cause (one sentence): filters (`cli`, `status`), pagination `limit`, sort `sort`/`dir`, and `autoRefresh` were held in `useState` only, so reload, deep-link, and browser back all silently reset the operator's view to defaults, and a URL like `/observability/sessions?cli=worker-cli&status=failure` was ignored. Fix: added `validateSearch` on the route with a strict Zod `SearchSchema` (whitelisted enums, `limit` bounded 1..500, booleans strictly typed), derived every UI value from `Route.useSearch()`, and pushed changes through `useNavigate({ from: Route.fullPath, replace: true })` inside a `patch()` helper that strips default values (`cli=""`, `limit=50`, `sort=StartedAt`, `dir=desc`, `autoRefresh=true`) so shared URLs stay short and canonical. Wired all setters through `patch`: dropdowns for `cli`/`status`, limit input, `toggleSort`, and the auto-refresh checkbox. Cursor pagination intentionally NOT in URL: cursors are opaque base64 blobs, per-query, would bloat history entries and are invalidated the moment filters change; reload restores filters + sort and re-fetches page 1, which is correct for a live-ops screen. Ancillary: kept `nextCursor` as component state, cleared by the reload effect on any search change, so a stale cursor can never leak into a new query. Verified: `bunx tsgo --noEmit` clean end-to-end. Version bump: v4.94.0 -> v4.95.0.

Plan 90 Step 85: **FE consumes `nextCursor` for "Load more" pagination in `SessionList` AND fixes a pre-existing envelope-shape bug across all three observability server-fns.** Root cause (one sentence): `src/lib/observability/{sessions,logs,ipc}.functions.ts` `EnvelopeSchema` read `env.Data`, but `BE/envelope.py:109` emits `Results: list[Any]` (spec Rule 1) and every list/detail route wraps its payload as `Results: [payload]`, so `env.Data` was always `undefined`, the handlers unconditionally threw `BE_HTTP_200`, and the observability UI has been surfacing the load-error banner for every real request since Step 75 - the exact silent-failure pattern the guidelines forbid. Minimum correct fix: rewrote the three envelope schemas to accept `Results: array` and `Errors` as either a single object or an array (matches both spec-legal shapes), extracted `Results[0]` as the payload, and parsed each endpoint's real payload with its dedicated Zod schema (a missing `Results[0]` now throws a loud `BE_ENVELOPE_EMPTY` error instead of a false 200). Added `cursor` (string, max 4096) to `getObservabilitySessions` `InputSchema` and outbound querystring; extended `PayloadSchema` with `sort`/`dir`/`nextCursor`. In `src/routes/observability.sessions.tsx` added `nextCursor` + `loadingMore` state, a `loadMore` callback that appends rows without wiping prior pages, and a "Load more / End of list" button below the table that is disabled while `!nextCursor`, `loadingMore`, or `loading`; the `load` callback still resets both `rows` and `nextCursor` on filter/sort/limit change so a cursor is never sent under a different query than it was minted for. Every failure path logs to `console.warn` with the cursor and BE error message (observability rule). Verified: `python -m pytest BE/tests/routes/test_observability_sessions.py` all 27 cases pass (envelope wire shape unchanged); `bunx tsgo --noEmit` clean end-to-end. Version bump: v4.93.0 -> v4.94.0.

Plan 90 Step 84: **Cursor pagination on `GET /observability/sessions`.** Root cause (one sentence): `_build_query` in `BE/routes/observability/sessions.py` emitted only `WHERE/ORDER BY/LIMIT ?` with no cursor, so once a fleet accrued more than 500 CLI invocations the oldest ones became unreachable under any sort even after Steps 82/83. Minimum correct fix: added an opaque base64url `cursor` query param carrying `{v, id, b?}` (bucket for `DurationMs` NULL-sink), a `_decode_cursor` that rejects malformed tokens with `E_BE_BAD_REQUEST` (never trusts caller JSON), a `_cursor_predicate` that emits `(expr <op> v) OR (expr = v AND CliInvocationId < id)` matching the existing `expr <dir>, CliInvocationId DESC` tiebreaker (with a bucket-aware branch: bucket 0 rows can be followed by more bucket 0 or any bucket 1, bucket 1 rows are only tiebroken by CliInvocationId), and `_next_cursor_from_row` that encodes the terminal row of a filled page. `nextCursor` is emitted in the response payload only when `len(rows) == limit_v` (partial page is EOF). Logged `cursor_in`/`cursor_out` on the `sessions_listed` line. FE untouched (Step 85 will consume `nextCursor`). Verified: `python -m pytest BE/tests/routes/test_observability_sessions.py` all 27 cases pass (4 new: null cursor on partial page, 7-row StartedAt desc walk with limit=3 covers full set with no dup/gap, DurationMs multi-page walk crossing the ended/active bucket boundary, invalid cursor 400). Version bump: v4.92.0 -> v4.93.0.

Plan 90 Step 83: **Wire FE `SessionList` sort/dir through the server function to BE.** Root cause (one sentence): `src/lib/observability/sessions.functions.ts` (`getObservabilitySessions`) never forwarded `sort`/`dir`, so the BE sort shipped in Step 82 was dead code and `observability.sessions.tsx` kept re-shuffling only the newest-500 slice returned by BE's default `StartedAt DESC`, hiding older rows that would rank higher under alternative orderings. Minimum correct fix: extended `InputSchema` with `sort` (`StartedAt|CliName|Status|DurationMs`) and `dir` (`asc|desc`) optionals, appended them to the outbound querystring, and updated `observability.sessions.tsx` to pass current `sortKey`/`sortDir` into `call({ data })` (added to the `useCallback` deps so a header click triggers a refetch), then removed the client-side `sortedRows` sort in favour of BE authority and deleted the now-dead `statusRank` helper (BE `_order_clause` owns the same ranking). Behaviour: sortable column clicks now hit BE, cover the whole eligible window (not just the newest 500), and preserve the running-rows-sink-last semantics contractually agreed with Step 82. Verified: `tsgo --noEmit` clean end-to-end; existing 23 BE pytest cases from Step 82 still authoritative for the ordering itself. Version bump: v4.91.0 -> v4.92.0.

## v4.91.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 82: **Server-side sort on `GET /observability/sessions`.** Root cause (one sentence): `_build_query` in `BE/routes/observability/sessions.py` hard-coded `ORDER BY StartedAt DESC` and accepted no sort input, so under the 500-row `LIMIT` any FE reordering (Step 81) only reshuffled the newest slice and hid older rows matching the sort. Minimum correct fix: added `sort` and `dir` query params with whitelists (`StartedAt|CliName|Status|DurationMs`, `asc|desc`), a `_order_clause` helper that (a) ranks `Status` active(0) < success(1) < failure(2) to match FE `statusRank`, (b) for `DurationMs` prepends `CASE WHEN EndedAt IS NULL THEN 1 ELSE 0 END ASC` so still-running rows always sink to the end regardless of direction (matching FE Step 81 semantics and keeping active work inside the 500-row window), and (c) appends a stable `CliInvocationId DESC` tiebreaker so identical values do not flap. Threaded validated inputs through `_build_query`, echoed `sort`/`dir` in the response payload and `sessions_listed` log line, and refreshed the docstring contract. Bad values raise `E_BE_BAD_REQUEST` with `Allowed` details. FE untouched (Step 81 client-side sort still works; server sort is additive). Verified: `python -m pytest BE/tests/routes/test_observability_sessions.py` all 23 cases pass (7 new: invalid sort/dir 400s, StartedAt asc, CliName asc, Status asc ranking, DurationMs NULL-sink both directions, response echoes sort/dir). Version bump: v4.90.0 -> v4.91.0.

## v4.84.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 75: **`useSessionLogTailAutoReconnect` (backoff policy layer over Step 74's SSE hook).** Root cause (one sentence): Step 74's `useSessionLogTail` fires `error` and closes the `EventSource` for good, so a long follow session dies silently on the first transient blip (BE restart, proxy 502, network flap) because no caller policy re-arms `reconnect()`. Minimum correct fix: new `src/lib/observability/useSessionLogTailAutoReconnect.ts` wraps `useSessionLogTail` and watches its status transitions. On `"error"` it schedules `reconnect()` after `min(baseMs * 2^attempt, maxMs)` (defaults 500 ms base, 15 s cap, 8 max attempts) via `setTimeout` scoped inside `useEffect` so nothing leaks across unmounts; on `"open"` it resets `attemptRef` and `consecutiveFailures` so a healed stream forgives past failures; on `"ended"` (clean BE `end` frame) it NEVER reconnects because end means the run finished, not that the stream broke. After `maxAttempts` consecutive failures without an intervening open it flips `gaveUp=true` and logs `[useSessionLogTailAutoReconnect] gave up` with `{runId, attempts, maxAttempts}`; every scheduled reconnect is `console.warn`ed with `{runId, attempt, delayMs}` context so silent failure is impossible. Exposes `reconnectCount` (successful re-arms since mount), `consecutiveFailures`, and `gaveUp` so Step 76's sonner drift toast has a stable trigger signal. Changing `runId` resets all policy state so switching sessions never inherits stale failure counts. Deliberately out of scope: sonner drift toast (Step 76), tail viewer wiring against the SSE hook (Step 76), UI for the `gaveUp` state (Step 76). Tests: new `src/lib/observability/__tests__/useSessionLogTailAutoReconnect.test.tsx` using vitest fake timers + injected `FakeEventSource` (transport error triggers a scheduled reconnect and increments `reconnectCount`; successful `open` after reconnect resets `consecutiveFailures`; clean `end` frame does NOT trigger any reconnect; `maxAttempts` consecutive failures set `gaveUp=true` and stop opening new sources). Verified: `bunx vitest run src/lib/observability/__tests__/useSessionLogTailAutoReconnect.test.tsx` -> **4 passed**. Version bump: v4.83.0 -> v4.84.0.

## v4.83.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 74: **`useSessionLogTail` React hook (browser client for the SSE tail proxy).** Root cause (one sentence): Step 73 delivered the same-origin SSE proxy but no React consumer existed, so the streaming path was reachable via `curl` but still unusable from the app UI. Minimum correct fix: new `src/lib/observability/useSessionLogTail.ts` opens `EventSource("/api/cli/sessions/{runId}/log?follow&since_line&max_lines")` inside a `useEffect` (SSR-safe, no top-level browser globals), collects default `message` frames into `lines[]` keyed by `EventSource.lastEventId` (so BE's 1-based line cursor round-trips as `Id`), parses the terminating `end` frame into `{RunId, LineCount, NextSinceLine, Truncated}` and flips `status` to `"ended"`, surfaces transport / named `error` frames as `status="error"` with the payload preview logged via `console.warn("[useSessionLogTail]", ...)`, and exposes a `reconnect()` seam that force-closes the current source and opens a fresh one with the current args (no built-in backoff; caller owns policy). Idle when `enabled=false` or `runId` is null; every state transition and every parse failure is logged with `runId` context so silent failure is impossible. Factory injection (`EventSourceFactory`) lets tests swap in a fake without patching globals. Deliberately out of scope: auto-reconnect + backoff (Step 75), sonner drift toasts (Step 76), tail viewer wiring against this hook (Step 76). Tests: new `src/lib/observability/__tests__/useSessionLogTail.test.tsx` (URL param encoding; idle-when-disabled; message frames + `end` parse; `error` -> status=error + close; `reconnect()` opens a second source and closes the first). Verified: `bunx vitest run src/lib/observability/__tests__/useSessionLogTail.test.tsx` -> **5 passed**, `[useSessionLogTail]` warn breadcrumb fires on the error path (verified in test spy). Version bump: v4.82.0 -> v4.83.0.

## v4.81.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 72: **SSE log tail `GET /api/cli/sessions/{run_id}/log`.** Root cause (one sentence): Step 71's disk-backed sessions list gave the UI a way to enumerate JSONL files but no way to actually read them, so any planned "open session" click would either dead-end or force the FE to reach for the DB-backed route that a wedged Root-DB writer would silently break. Minimum correct fix: (1) new `_find_session_by_run_id(log_root, run_id)` in `BE/routes/cli_observability.py` reuses `list_sessions(limit=500)` so an unknown RunId cannot force an unbounded directory walk; (2) new `_tail_stream(...)` async generator opens the file once, tracks a 1-based `current_line` cursor, honors `since_line` + `max_lines` (`1..5000`, default 1000), emits SSE `data:` frames with `id:` line numbers so the browser's `EventSource.lastEventId` is a resumable cursor, and terminates with `event: end` carrying `{RunId, LineCount, NextSinceLine, Truncated}`; (3) `follow=true` polls at 250 ms for at most 30 s so a wedged client cannot pin a worker thread; (4) unknown RunId raises `AppError(E_BE_NOT_FOUND)` with `Details.RunId` so operators can distinguish "never existed" from "rotated away" in the standard envelope; (5) file-open failure between lookup and stream open is surfaced as an `event: error` SSE frame carrying `{Code:"E_BE_INTERNAL", Reason}` rather than a silent stream close, with a matching `cli_sessions_tail.open_failed` warning log; (6) response headers set `Cache-Control: no-cache, no-transform` and `X-Accel-Buffering: no` so upstream proxies don't buffer the tail. ## v4.80.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 142: **Reverse envelope adapter + `GET /rules/{id}/set` round-trip.** Root cause (one sentence): Step 139's `projectRulesetToEnvelope` was one-way, so the reload-server conflict resolver had nothing to rebind `useRulesStore` from, and `loadRuleSet` was pointed at `/rules/{id}` which returns a `CatRule` (not a `RuleSetEnvelope`), meaning every reload-server code path silently type-coerced the wrong shape and would corrupt editor state the first time an operator hit "Reload from server" on a real conflict. Minimum correct fix: (1) new `envelopeToProjectRuleset(envelope, {projectId, categoryName?, rulesetId?})` in `src/lib/rules/envelopeAdapter.ts` rehydrates every `_Legacy*` field the forward adapter stashed (`_LegacyId`, `_LegacyKind`, `_Rotation`, `_Family`, `_SourceRuleId`, `_IsHidden`, `_IsLocked`) back onto `EditorRule`, deletes those namespaced keys from `Params` so no leakage back into the editor, falls back to wire `Kind` (`presence -> R`, `absence -> S`, `measure -> E`, else `C`) with a `UnknownKindFallbacks` counter logged, and reverses `RuleSetId` via `fromRulesetIntId` with a deterministic `rs-{intId}` fallback so envelopes minted on another device still mount; (2) new `RuleFacade.get_rule_set(rule_set_id) -> RuleSetEnvelope` on both `InMemoryRuleFacade` and `VendorRuleFacade` (raises `E_BE_NOT_FOUND` on unknown id, `E_SDK_INIT_FAILED` on unconfigured vendor) so the Protocol self-check at import stays green; (3) new `GET /rules/{id}/set` route in `BE/routes/rules.py` returning the committed envelope wrapped in the standard success envelope, distinct URL from `GET /rules/{id}` so the two wire shapes never overload the same path; (4) `src/lib/rules/loadRuleSet.ts` retargeted from `/rules/{id}` to `/rules/{id}/set` so the reload-server resolver + `reconcileDrafts` now hit the correct endpoint. Deliberately out of scope: no SQLite `RuleFacade` (in-memory still authoritative for provider parity; SQLite lands with Step 143 alongside `SampleFacade`); no `Version` CAS on the BE side (already implemented in `save_rule_set`; contract test covers commit path). Tests: new `BE/tests/test_rules_roundtrip.py` (PUT then GET returns byte-identical envelope with server-owned `Version`/`Origin`/`UpdatedAt` stamps + `_Legacy*` fields intact; missing id -> 404 `E_BE_NOT_FOUND`), new `src/lib/rules/__tests__/envelopeRoundtrip.test.ts` (forward+back preserves every lossless field; wire-Kind fallback when `_LegacyKind` stripped; deterministic `rs-{intId}` recovery without an alias table). Verified: `bunx tsgo --noEmit` clean; `bunx vitest run src/lib/rules/__tests__/envelopeRoundtrip.test.ts` -> **3 passed**; `envelopeAdapter.test.ts`, `reconcileDrafts.test.ts`, `bootReconcile.test.ts` still green. BE pytest not runnable in this sandbox (no fastapi in env), test structure mirrors existing passing `test_rules_save.py`. Not fixed (deliberate): editor's reload-server resolver still uses `loadRuleSet` -> envelope only; wiring `envelopeToProjectRuleset` into `useRulesStore.replaceAll` at the conflict-modal call site lands with the samples/audits work in Steps 143+. Minimum correct fix: (1) new `src/lib/rules/ruleset-id-alias.ts` mirrors `rule-id-alias.ts` byte-for-byte -- `toRulesetIntId(rulesetId: string): number` assigns a stable positive integer alias to each ruleset string id in `localStorage` under `ca.rulesetIdAlias.v1`, and `fromRulesetIntId(intId: number): string | null` provides the reverse lookup so a future boot-toast deep-link (Step 141) can navigate from an envelope `RuleSetId` back to the router's string `rulesetId`; (2) new `src/lib/rules/envelopeAdapter.ts` exports `projectRulesetToEnvelope(ruleset, opts?): { envelope, droppedCategories }` which converts a legacy `RuleSet` into a wire-ready `RuleSetEnvelope`: EditorRuleKind letters map deterministically per a documented provisional table (`C, K -> match`, `R -> presence`, `S -> absence`, `E -> measure`) with unknown letters logged and skipped rather than silently defaulted; `isCategory: true` rows are stripped and the count reported so the caller can surface it; lossy fields (raw kind letter, rotation, family, sourceRuleId, isHidden, isLocked) are preserved verbatim under namespaced `_Legacy*` keys in `RuleItem.Params` so Step 142's reverse mapping is lossless and no editor state is silently dropped on save; `RuleItem.Enabled` mirrors `!isHidden` so runtime honors legacy visibility; `Shape` is `{Type:"rect", X, Y, W, H}` and `Tolerance` defaults to `{Kind:"pct", Value:5}` (both documented as provisional); `opts.version` is caller-supplied so the Save flow owns optimistic-lock semantics (defaults to 0 for a never-saved ruleset); `ClientId` is minted once per browser under `ca.envelopeClientId.v1` via `crypto.randomUUID` (with a `c-<timestamp>` fallback for jsdom); every serialize call logs one structured `[envelopeAdapter] serialized { RuleSetId, LegacyRuleSetId, Rules, DroppedCategories, Version }` line so silent field loss is architecturally impossible. Deliberately out of scope: no reverse `envelopeToProjectRuleset` (Step 142 owns it alongside the SQLite `RuleFacade` -- that is when server-committed envelopes first re-enter the editor); no editor Save-button mount (Step 140 mounts `SaveRuleSetButton` into `RulesetHeader.actionsSlot` and feeds it the adapter output); no boot-toast deep-link (Step 141 wires `fromRulesetIntId` into a sonner `toast.warning` action). Tests: new `src/lib/rules/__tests__/envelopeAdapter.test.ts` (jsdom) with 6 cases: emits wire-valid envelope with stable integer ids, maps every EditorRuleKind letter deterministically, strips categories and reports drop count, preserves lossy fields under `_Legacy*` for reverse mapping (including `_IsHidden` + `Enabled` mirror), assigns stable `RuleSetId` across two invocations for the same ruleset, `opts.version` passes through so callers own optimistic-lock. Verified: `bunx vitest run src/lib/rules/__tests__/envelopeAdapter.test.ts` -> **6 passed**, and `[envelopeAdapter] serialized` fires on every call (verified in test stdout). ## v4.78.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 138: **Shell boot mount for `reconcileDrafts()` + auto-surface drift to the operator.** Root cause (one sentence): `reconcileDrafts()` shipped in Step 137 was an unreachable seam because no shell code invoked it on mount, so any cross-tab or cross-operator commit left the local IndexedDB draft silently stale until the operator hit Save (or never, if the ruleset was deleted server-side). Minimum correct fix: (1) new browser seam `src/lib/rules/bootReconcile.ts` exporting `runBootReconcile(opts?): Promise<DraftReconcileEntry[]>` with a module-level `bootRan` flag (once-per-page-load, StrictMode-safe) and a `bootInFlight` promise ref (concurrent callers share the single call, no double-fetch storm on React double-render); (2) on each `server-newer` and `local-newer` entry the hook emits one sonner `toast.warning` titled `Rule set #<id> is out of sync with the server` with description `Local draft vX, server vY. Open the rule set to reload or overwrite.` so the operator sees drift immediately at boot instead of on the next Save; (3) `server-missing` and `load-failed` intentionally do not toast (they are already surfaced by `reconcileDrafts`'s per-entry structured log; auto-purging or scaring the operator at boot would either discard local work or spam on transient network hiccups); (4) after every pass the module emits one structured `[bootReconcile] complete { total, drift, missing, failed }` `console.info` line so operators can grep boot health; (5) the whole reconcile call is wrapped in try/catch that logs `[bootReconcile] fatal` and flips `bootRan = true` (no retry-storm) so a broken IDB or offline network cannot brick the shell; (6) the toast notifier is injected via `opts.notify` so tests can assert exact call-count without spinning up sonner. Wired into `src/routes/__root.tsx` next to the existing error-history hydrate effect (new import + one `useEffect(() => { void runBootReconcile(); }, [])`). Deliberately out of scope: no editor drawer mount for `SaveRuleSetButton` (Step 139, needs an adapter between `RuleSetEnvelope` and the legacy `EditorRule`/`useRulesStore` shape so clicking the toast can deep-link into the conflict resolver); no auto-open of `SaveConflictModal` from the toast click (Step 139 owns that once the drawer mount lands); no SQLite `RuleFacade` (Step 140). Tests: new `src/lib/rules/__tests__/bootReconcile.test.ts` (vitest) with 4 cases: idempotent (`reconcileDrafts` called exactly once across three invocations, module flag reset via test-only helper), concurrent invocations dedupe to a single in-flight call, notifier fires exactly twice for a fixture with 5 entries (2 drift, 1 in-sync, 1 missing, 1 failed) and skips non-drift kinds, `reconcileDrafts` rejection returns `[]` and logs `[bootReconcile] fatal` (never rethrows). Verified: `bunx vitest run src/lib/rules/__tests__/bootReconcile.test.ts` -> **4 passed**, and the `[bootReconcile] complete` structured line fires on every run (verified in test stdout). Not fixed (deliberate): editor drawer mount + toast deep-link (Step 139); SQLite `RuleFacade` (Step 140). Version bump: v4.77.0 -> v4.78.0.

## v4.77.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 137: **`reconcileDrafts()` boot reconciliation for locally-cached RuleSet drafts.** Root cause (one sentence): Steps 132-136 gave the editor a per-ruleset draft -> save -> conflict-modal loop but nothing runs when the browser reopens, so if another operator committed changes while this tab was closed the local IndexedDB draft silently diverges and the operator only discovers it on the next Save (or never, if the ruleset was deleted server-side). Minimum correct fix: new browser seam `src/lib/rules/reconcileDrafts.ts` exporting `reconcileDrafts(opts?): Promise<DraftReconcileEntry[]>` that (1) enumerates every local draft via `listDraftIds()`, (2) `GET /rules/{id}` via `loadRuleSet` (which also mirrors the server envelope back into IDB with `Origin: "server"`), (3) classifies each entry into one of five documented kinds: `in-sync` (Version equal), `server-newer` (conflict; must resolve), `local-newer` (should not happen; treated as conflict so the modal fires), `server-missing` (404; optional `purgeMissing` deletes the dead draft), `load-failed` (transport / non-404 error; draft left alone so a flaky GET never nukes local work), (4) never throws so a single failed GET does not block boot, (5) logs one structured line per entry (`console.info` for `in-sync`, `console.warn` with `{ RuleSetId, Kind, LocalVersion, ServerVersion, Error }` for every drift kind) so silent divergence is architecturally impossible. Deliberately out of scope: no shell boot mount (Step 138 wires `reconcileDrafts()` into the app root and auto-opens `SaveConflictModal` for each `server-newer` entry); no editor drawer mount for `SaveRuleSetButton` (deferred, requires an adapter between `RuleSetEnvelope` and the existing v3 `EditorRule`/`useRulesStore` shape); no SQLite `RuleFacade`. Tests: new `src/lib/rules/__tests__/reconcileDrafts.test.ts` (jsdom) with 4 cases: five-way classification (in-sync, server-newer, local-newer, server-missing, load-failed), `purgeMissing` deletes 404 drafts, transport `fetch` reject becomes `load-failed` (never throws), empty draft list short-circuits with no GETs. Verified: `bunx vitest run src/lib/rules/__tests__/reconcileDrafts.test.ts` -> **4 passed**, and the `[reconcileDrafts]` structured log line fires on every drift entry (verified in test stderr). ## v4.70.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 130: **Rollback-on-critical-failure module + `bin/install-rollback.py` CLI.** Root cause guarded (one sentence): Step 129 shipped the upgrade planner + manifest backup end-to-end but nothing consumed the `BackupPath` when a subsequent CRITICAL install action failed, so a half-applied upgrade left `install.json` recording partial success with no supported path back to the prior good state. Minimum correct fix: (1) new pure module `BE/app/installer_rollback.py` exposing frozen `RollbackDecision(FailedAction, ActionsToReverse, PriorVersion, CurrentVersion, BackupPath)`, `plan_rollback(*, current, backup, failed_action, backup_path)` which returns actions present in `current` but not `backup` (identified by `(Name, StartedAt)` tuples, ordered newest-first for strict LIFO reversal), `restore_manifest(install_root, backup_path)` (atomic same-dir tmp + `os.replace`), and `load_backup(backup_path)` (wraps `read_manifest` into the rollback error surface); (2) one new registered error code `E_INSTALL_ROLLBACK_FAILED` -> 503 SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE covering missing/unreadable/invalid backup, diverged history, empty `failed_action`, and atomic-write failure; (3) new CLI `bin/install-rollback.py` with `--install-root`, `--backup-path`, `--failed-action`, `--dry-run` printing single-line PascalCase JSON `{FailedAction, ActionsToReverse, PriorVersion, CurrentVersion, BackupPath, RestoredPath, DryRun}` with exit contract 0 / 2 / 50. Deliberate scope: matches the Step 126/128 pattern (ship pure module + CLI now, wire wrappers next step); the actual reversal of side-effects (shims, scheduled tasks, DB rows) is Step 132 and consumes `ActionsToReverse` directly. Refuses to rollback against diverged history rather than silently reversing wrong entries. Tests: new `BE/tests/app/test_installer_rollback.py` with 16 cases: 4 planner (LIFO ordering + version fields, empty when no new actions, empty-failed-action rejection, diverged-history rejection), 4 `restore_manifest` (successful atomic replace, missing backup, invalid JSON, non-object root), 3 `load_backup` (successful read, missing, wrapped manifest error), 5 CLI subprocess (rollback exit 0 + on-disk restore verified, `--dry-run` leaves disk untouched, missing backup exit 50 + typed name on stderr, missing current manifest exit 50, diverged history exit 50 with `diverged` in message). Verified: `pytest BE/tests/app/test_installer_rollback.py -q` -> **16 passed**. Full `BE/tests/app/` suite: **581 passed, 1 skipped** (was 565; +16 net). Not fixed (deliberate scope): no wrapper wiring of `install-rollback.py` into the per-action failure path (Step 131); no reversal of the failed action's on-disk side effects (Step 132); no ACL hardening (Step 133); no idempotent Scheduled Task re-register on version bump (Step 134); no Authenticode signing (Step 141+). Version bump: v4.69.0 -> v4.70.0.

## v4.69.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 129: **Wire `bin/install-upgrade-plan.py` into both installer wrappers between the doctor and the plan renderer, and add `--force-reinstall` / `--allow-downgrade` wrapper flags with a dedicated exit-code range.** Root cause guarded (one sentence): Step 128 shipped the pure planner + CLI + 26 tests but nothing between the doctor and the plan renderer dispatched it, so a repeat `--install` still had no typed decision layer and could silently overwrite a newer install with an older one. Minimum correct fix: (1) `packaging/installers/install.sh` gains `--force-reinstall` and `--allow-downgrade` bash flags plus an INSTALL-only block right after the doctor that invokes `$PYTHON_EXE $REPO_ROOT/bin/install-upgrade-plan.py --install-root "$INSTALL_ROOT" --new-version "$APP_VERSION_STR" --backup [--force-reinstall] [--allow-downgrade]`, captures the JSON decision on stdout, and maps the Step-128 CLI exit contract to fresh wrapper exit codes: 40 (downgrade blocked) -> 7, 41 (invalid version or corrupt manifest) -> 8, 42 (manifest backup unwritable) -> 9; skipped on `--uninstall` because there is no version comparison to make; (2) `packaging/installers/install.ps1` gains `[switch]$ForceReinstall` and `[switch]$AllowDowngrade` param switches and reserves three new wrapper-only exit codes in the 9500-9599 range (`$EXIT_DOWNGRADE_BLOCKED = 9534`, `$EXIT_UPGRADE_INVALID = 9535`, `$EXIT_BACKUP_UNWRITABLE = 9536`) with the same 40/41/42 -> 9534/9535/9536 mapping, forwarding the flags only when set so the CLI never receives spurious arguments; (3) both wrappers place the upgrade planner AFTER the doctor (so a broken preflight aborts before we touch `install.json`) and BEFORE the plan renderer and per-action recorder loop (so a blocked downgrade never records anything and never mutates the DB); (4) `install.sh` header comment enumerates the new exit codes 7/8/9 alongside the existing 2-6 so operators reading `--help` output see the full contract. Tests: new `BE/tests/app/test_installer_wrapper_upgrade_wiring.py` with 11 contract tests locking the wiring on both wrappers - `install.sh` declares the two new flags with zero-init counters, invokes the CLI with `--install-root`, `--new-version`, `--backup`, maps 40/41/42 -> 7/8/9 via the `case` block, phase-guards the block with `if [ "$phase" = "install" ]`, orders it strictly between the `cat /tmp/install-doctor.out` marker and the `plan_json=$(` marker, and documents 7/8/9 in the header block; `install.ps1` declares `[switch]$ForceReinstall` / `[switch]$AllowDowngrade`, reserves 9534/9535/9536, invokes the CLI via `(Join-Path $RepoRoot 'bin/install-upgrade-plan.py')` with `--install-root`, `--new-version`, `--backup`, maps 40/41/42 to `$EXIT_DOWNGRADE_BLOCKED` / `$EXIT_UPGRADE_INVALID` / `$EXIT_BACKUP_UNWRITABLE`, phase-guards with `if ($phase -eq 'install')`, orders the block strictly between `$doctorExit = $LASTEXITCODE` and `$planJson = & $VenvPython -c $planScript`, and forwards the flags only when the switches are set. The upgrade CLI itself is already exercised end-to-end by the Step-128 subprocess suite (`test_installer_upgrade.py`), so contract-level source tests are the correct depth here - shelling `install.sh` would require the full `.venv`, a fabricated release directory with `SHA256SUMS.txt`, and the doctor's wrapper inventory - none of which are in scope for a wiring step. Verified: `python3 -m pytest BE/tests/app/test_installer_wrapper_upgrade_wiring.py BE/tests/app/test_installer_upgrade.py -q` -> **37 passed** (11 new + 26 pre-existing). Full `BE/tests/app/` suite: **565 passed, 1 skipped** (was 554; +11 net). Not fixed (deliberate scope): no rollback-on-critical-failure that consumes the `BackupPath` emitted by the CLI's `--backup` (Step 130); no `%ProgramData%\vision-app` / `/var/lib/vision-app` ACL hardening (Step 131); no idempotent Scheduled Task re-register on version bump (Step 132); no Pester coverage of the new exit codes on `windows-latest` (Step 133+); no Authenticode signing (Step 141). Version bump: v4.68.0 -> v4.69.0.

Plan 90 Step 128: **Upgrade-in-place decision planner + manifest backup + `bin/install-upgrade-plan.py` CLI.** Root cause guarded (one sentence): Steps 105-127 shipped install and uninstall but no path decided whether a second `--install` against a populated `install.json` was a legal upgrade, a same-version reinstall, or an illegal downgrade, so any repeat run either silently overwrote a newer install with an older one or crashed on the `init_manifest` idempotency guard with no operator-actionable signal. Minimum correct fix: (1) new pure module `BE/app/installer_upgrade.py` exposing `UpgradeAction` enum (`FRESH_INSTALL` / `UPGRADE` / `REINSTALL_SAME` / `DOWNGRADE_ALLOWED`), `UpgradePolicy(is_force_reinstall, is_downgrade_allowed)`, frozen `UpgradeDecision(Action, PriorVersion, NewVersion, Reason)`, `parse_version(raw) -> (major, minor, patch)` (accepts `4.67.0` / `v4.67` / `4` / `4.67.0-rc.1` / `4.67.0+build.5`, rejects `1.2.3.4`, `1..2`, `abc`, empty), `plan_upgrade(*, existing, new_version, policy)` (FRESH when no manifest, UPGRADE when new>prior, REINSTALL_SAME when equal with distinct `Reason` for `--force-reinstall`, DOWNGRADE_ALLOWED when new<prior + `is_downgrade_allowed`, raises `E_INSTALL_DOWNGRADE_BLOCKED` otherwise), and `backup_manifest(install_root, *, now)` that copies `install.json` -> `install.json.bak.<UTC-ISO>` via `shutil.copy2`, returning the path or None on fresh install, raising `E_INSTALL_MANIFEST_UNWRITABLE` on copy failure and `E_INSTALL_MANIFEST_INVALID` on naive-tz timestamp; (2) two new registered error codes in `BE/errors/codes.py`: `E_INSTALL_DOWNGRADE_BLOCKED` -> 409 Conflict (a downgrade is a conflict against the recorded newer state) and `E_INSTALL_UPGRADE_INVALID` -> 422 Unprocessable (a malformed version string is an unprocessable payload); (3) new CLI `bin/install-upgrade-plan.py` with `--install-root`, `--new-version`, `--force-reinstall`, `--allow-downgrade`, `--backup` printing a single-line PascalCase JSON decision (`{Action, PriorVersion, NewVersion, Reason, ForceReinstall, AllowDowngrade, BackupPath}`) so wrappers can shell-parse without pulling the module. Exit-code contract: 0 (decision emitted), 2 (usage), 40 (downgrade blocked), 41 (invalid version string OR unreadable/malformed manifest), 42 (backup unwritable). Deliberate scope: matches the Step-126 pattern (Step ships CLI+module, next step wires the wrapper); `install.sh`/`install.ps1` still run without this gate for one more step, then Step 129 threads it in ahead of the plan renderer. Tests: new `BE/tests/app/test_installer_upgrade.py` covers 7 parse-version cases (6 valid shapes parametrised + 5 invalid parametrised), 7 planner cases (fresh, upgrade, reinstall-same default reason, reinstall-same force reason, downgrade blocked, downgrade allowed, invalid new-version), 3 backup cases (None when missing, stamped copy with content preserved, naive-tz rejection), and 5 CLI subprocess cases (fresh exit 0, upgrade+backup emits path and file exists, downgrade blocked exit 40 with typed error name on stderr, downgrade allowed exit 0, invalid version exit 41 with typed error name); subprocess helper sets `PYTHONPATH=REPO_ROOT` matching the Step-126 CLI test pattern. Verified: `python3 -m pytest BE/tests/app/test_installer_upgrade.py -q` -> **26 passed**. Full `BE/tests/app/` suite: **554 passed, 1 skipped** (was 528 before Step 128; +26 new). Not fixed (deliberate scope): no wrapper wiring of `install-upgrade-plan.py` into `install.sh`/`install.ps1` ahead of the plan renderer (Step 129); no rollback-on-critical-failure using the backup (Step 130); no `%ProgramData%\vision-app` / `/var/lib/vision-app` ACL hardening (Step 131); no Pester coverage of upgrade contract on `windows-latest` (Step 132+); no Authenticode signing (Step 141). Version bump: v4.67.0 -> v4.68.0.

Plan 90 Step 127: **Wire the Step-126 PATH-link CLI into `BE/app/installer_plan.py`, both installer wrappers, `bin/install-doctor.py`, and the manifest audit trail.** Root cause guarded (one sentence): Step 126 shipped `bin/install-path-link.py` as a standalone CLI but nothing dispatched it between `db-bootstrap` and `retention-timer`, so a fresh install still stranded the onefile `.exe` / ELF payloads off-PATH and an uninstall never removed the shims. Minimum correct fix: (1) `plan_install_actions` grows a `binaries_dir: str | None = None` kwarg and a new `_path_link_action` helper that emits `bin/install-path-link.py install --binaries-dir <dir> --platform {windows,posix}` on INSTALL (critical=True) and `bin/install-path-link.py uninstall --platform ...` on UNINSTALL (critical=False so an already-removed shim can never block schema teardown); (2) INSTALL order is now `[db-bootstrap, path-link, retention-timer]` so shims exist before the scheduled task registers, UNINSTALL order is the mirror `[retention-timer, path-link, db-bootstrap]` so the loop halts before the shims/schema it references vanish; (3) INSTALL phase without `binaries_dir` raises `AppError(E_CLI_USAGE)` with an explicit message tying the parameter to the path-link source-exe resolution; (4) `packaging/installers/install.sh` and `install.ps1` both forward the resolved binaries dir into the planner heredoc so the single `$BINARIES_DIR` / `-BinariesDir` value drives both the SHA256 cross-check (Steps 124/125) and shim materialization (Step 126) - Step 125's `binaries_dir` guard already refuses install without it, so the planner's INSTALL contract is enforced at both layers; (5) `bin/install-doctor.py` grows an optional `--binaries-dir` flag (both wrappers pass it through via `${BINARIES_DIR:+--binaries-dir "$BINARIES_DIR"}` / `if ($BinariesDir) { $doctorArgs += ... }`) and falls back to a `<unset>` placeholder on install so preflight still runs when a dev-loop caller omits it - the wrapper still refuses to proceed without an explicit binaries dir; (6) because the wrapper loop already routes every action through `bin/install-record.py`, `path-link` rows appear in `install.json` with zero changes to the recorder. Tests: `BE/tests/app/test_installer_plan.py` updated (order assertions now include `"path-link"`, callers pass `binaries_dir=...` where INSTALL is used) and 5 new cases lock the path-link contract (missing `binaries_dir` raises `E_CLI_USAGE`; Windows + POSIX install args include the resolved dir and platform flag with `critical=True`; Windows + POSIX uninstall args omit the dir, carry `--platform`, and `critical=False`). Doctor test helper `_plan()` updated with `binaries_dir="/tmp/fake-release"`. Verified: `python3 -m pytest BE/tests/app/test_installer_plan.py BE/tests/app/test_installer_doctor_and_record.py -q` -> **38 passed** (was 30 in the plan file; +8 net: 5 new + 3 existing generalized). Not fixed (deliberate scope): no upgrade-in-place / `--force-reinstall` (Step 128); no `%ProgramData%` ACL hardening (Step 129); no idempotent Scheduled Task re-register on version bump (Step 130); no Pester coverage of the new plan shape on `windows-latest` (Step 131+); no Authenticode signing (Step 141). Version bump: v4.66.0 -> v4.67.0.

Plan 90 Step 126: **PATH-link lifecycle module + CLI for shipped PyInstaller binaries.** Root cause guarded (one sentence): Steps 117-125 built and verified the onefile `.exe` payloads but left them stranded in an out-of-PATH release directory, so an operator finishing `install.ps1` still had to type an absolute path to run `db-bootstrap` / `retention-run` and any second install silently shadowed the previous shim without a recorded uninstall trail. Minimum correct fix: (1) new pure module `BE/app/installer_path.py` exposing `LinkPlatform` enum, frozen `LinkAction(Name, Source, LinkPath)`, `default_link_dir(platform)` (Windows -> `%LOCALAPPDATA%\vision-app\bin`, POSIX -> `~/.local/share/vision-app/bin`), pure planner `plan_link_actions(*, platform, binaries_dir, link_dir, binaries=BINARIES)` that iterates the frozen `BE.app.installer_binaries.BINARIES` inventory so a new entry auto-participates, plus thin I/O entry points `apply_link_install` / `apply_link_uninstall` that raise `AppError(E_INSTALL_PATH_LINK_FAILED)` on every failure path; (2) Windows install writes a `<ExeName>.cmd` shim (`@echo off` + `@call "<abs-source>" %*`) via atomic same-dir tmp + `os.replace` because `os.symlink` on Windows requires Developer Mode or admin, POSIX install materializes a `<ExeName>` symlink via `os.symlink` + `os.replace` for idempotent replacement; (3) uninstall is idempotent (missing shim is a no-op, never raises) but refuses to remove a directory or special file at the link path (defensive: it is not ours to remove); (4) new error code `E_INSTALL_PATH_LINK_FAILED` in `BE/errors/codes.py` mapping to HTTP 503 (infra unavail, matches log/manifest root); (5) new CLI `bin/install-path-link.py` with `install` / `uninstall` subcommands, `--binaries-dir` (required on install), `--link-dir` (defaults to `default_link_dir`), `--platform {windows,posix}` (auto-detects on omit), numeric exit contract `0` = ok, `2` = `E_CLI_USAGE`, `30` = `E_INSTALL_PATH_LINK_FAILED`, stderr markers `[<code>] <msg>` for grep-ability. Verified: new `BE/tests/app/test_installer_path.py` (13 cases: Windows planner uses `.cmd` + `.exe` names, POSIX planner uses bare names, non-Path arg -> `E_CLI_USAGE`, `default_link_dir` reads `LOCALAPPDATA` on Windows and `HOME` on POSIX, POSIX install materializes symlink + is idempotent on re-run, Windows install writes the `@call` shim body with the exact source path, missing source -> `E_INSTALL_PATH_LINK_FAILED`, uninstall is idempotent, uninstall refuses to remove a directory, plus 3 CLI cases locking exit `0` / `30` / `2`) -> **13 passed**. Not fixed (deliberate scope): the plan renderer in `installer_plan.py` and both installer wrappers (`install.ps1`, `install.sh`) do not yet dispatch `install-path-link.py` between `db-bootstrap` and `retention-timer` (Step 127); no user-PATH env-var mutation (never in scope for this module; a follow-up in Step 128 will document the manual `$Env:Path` / `.profile` edit); no manifest recording of the link actions (Step 127 threads them through `bin/install-record.py`); no upgrade-in-place logic (Step 128); no `%ProgramData%` ACL hardening (Step 129). Version bump: v4.65.0 -> v4.66.0.

## v4.65.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 125: **`verify-install` CI gate + removal of the SHA256SUMS "skipped" fallback from `packaging/installers/install.sh` and `install.ps1`.** Root cause guarded (one sentence): Step 124 wired both installers through `bin/install-verify-sums.py` but nothing in CI actually ran `install.sh` / `install.ps1` end-to-end against a fabricated release directory, so a broken pre-doctor checksum block (missing exit translation, wrong flag, silent skip fallback re-introduced) would only be caught by a human on a Windows box. Minimum correct fix: (1) new `.github/workflows/verify-install.yml` with `concurrency: verify-install-${{ github.ref }} cancel-in-progress: true` and two OS jobs (`posix` on `ubuntu-latest`, `windows` on `windows-latest`) triggered by `pull_request` (paths-scoped to installers + verify CLI + workflow) and `push: branches: [main]`; (2) each job fabricates a fake release dir whose exe payloads match `BE.app.installer_binaries.BINARIES` (POSIX leg -> no suffix, GNU text-mode sums; Windows leg -> `.exe` suffix, GNU binary-mode `*name` sums) so the parser gets both grammars exercised on every PR; (3) three assertions per job — HAPPY (`--verify-only` / `-VerifyOnly` exits `0`), TAMPERED (one byte flipped -> `6` on bash / `9533` on ps1), MISSING BINARIES DIR (`--install` / `-Install` without `APP_BINARIES_DIR` -> `2`); (4) `install.sh` gains a `--verify-only` flag that short-circuits after the checksum block, adds `[ "$phase" = "install" ] && [ -z "$BINARIES_DIR" ] -> exit 2` guard, updates usage/exit-code docs; (5) `install.ps1` gains a `-VerifyOnly` `[switch]`, a `$EXIT_USAGE = 2` constant, and the mirror guard `if ($phase -eq 'install' -and -not $BinariesDir) { exit 2 }`; (6) the Step-124 "skipped when unset" log branches are removed from both scripts so every install goes through the cross-check (uninstall still tolerates an empty binaries dir - no new bytes are laid down); (7) the Windows job provisions a stub `.venv/Scripts/python.exe` so the early `$VenvPython` existence check does not fire `9530` before the checksum block runs. Tests: new `BE/tests/app/test_verify_install_workflow.py` (6 cases) - workflow parses under `pyyaml`, both `posix` and `windows` jobs assert the HAPPY / TAMPERED / MISSING triad in their step names, both installers stripped of the `cross-check skipped` string and now carry the mandatory guards + verify-only flag, plus a bash end-to-end smoke that fabricates a release dir and asserts `0` / `6` / `2` from `install.sh` for the three cases. Verified: `python3 -m pytest BE/tests/app/test_verify_install_workflow.py -q` -> **6 passed**; local dry-run against `install.sh` with a fabricated `/tmp/ci-release` -> HAPPY `exit=0`, TAMPERED `exit=6`, MISSING `exit=2`; verify-sums CLI stderr carries the truncation-immune `[13] E_CLI_CHECKSUM_MISMATCH` marker on tamper. Not fixed (deliberate scope): tag-trigger in `release.yml` is still commented out (flip in Step 200 with the v5.0.0 cut once signing lands in Step 141); no Authenticode signing overlay yet; no PATH junction / uninstall path (Steps 126-130). Version bump: v4.64.0 -> v4.65.0.

Plan 90 Step 124: **`SHA256SUMS.txt` cross-check inside `packaging/installers/install.ps1` and `install.sh`.** Root cause guarded (one sentence): Step 123 published `SHA256SUMS.txt` alongside the PyInstaller onefile artefacts but neither installer opened it, so a tampered `db-bootstrap.exe` / `retention-run.exe` swapped into `%APP_BINARIES_DIR%` between download and install would only be caught post-install by Step 118's doctor tamper check, after files were already laid down and `install.json` rows recorded. Minimum correct fix: (1) new pure module `BE/app/installer_checksums.py` exposing `parse_sums_file(path) -> dict[str, str]`, `verify_release_binaries(*, sums_path, binaries_dir, platform, inventory=BINARIES) -> list[VerifiedBinary]`, and the frozen `VerifiedBinary(Name, ExeFilename, Sha256, SizeBytes)` audit row; (2) parser accepts BOTH GNU text mode (`<hex><space><space><filename>`) and binary mode (`<hex><space>*<filename>`) via one regex so the Windows leg (PowerShell `Get-FileHash`) and Linux leg (`sha256sum`) from Step 123 converge on one file format, skips blank + `#`-comment lines, refuses duplicate filenames with 1-based line-number `details`; (3) `verify_release_binaries` iterates `BE.app.installer_binaries.BINARIES` so a new frozen `BinaryEntry` auto-participates with zero installer edits, re-uses Step-118's streaming `installer_signing.sha256_of_file` so the pre-install verifier and the post-install doctor tamper check compute hashes identically; (4) failure taxonomy maps sums-missing -> `E_INSTALL_MANIFEST_MISSING`, malformed / duplicate / inventory-missing-from-sums -> `E_INSTALL_MANIFEST_INVALID`, binary-file-missing -> `E_INSTALL_MANIFEST_MISSING` with `Reason=binary-file-missing`, digest mismatch -> `E_CLI_CHECKSUM_MISMATCH` (spec/21-app/77 §4); (5) new `bin/install-verify-sums.py` CLI takes `--sums-path`, `--binaries-dir`, `--platform {windows,posix}`, optional `--json`, classifies AppError -> exit codes 10 (sums missing) / 11 (invalid) / 12 (binary absent) / 13 (mismatch), prints truncation-immune `[NN] <ERROR_CODE>: <msg>` markers to stderr plus a sorted `details` JSON line, and 0 on success with one `[install-verify-sums] ok <file> <sha>` line per accepted artefact; (6) `packaging/installers/install.ps1` gains `-BinariesDir` param (defaults to `$env:APP_BINARIES_DIR`), a new `$EXIT_CHECKSUM_MISMATCH=9533` constant, and a pre-doctor block that shells out to `bin/install-verify-sums.py --platform windows`, streams stderr, and collapses any non-zero verify exit to `9533` with a `[9533] SHA256SUMS cross-check failed (verify-sums exit <n>)` marker; (7) `packaging/installers/install.sh` gains `BINARIES_DIR` from `APP_BINARIES_DIR`, a new `exit 6` mapping (`[6] SHA256SUMS cross-check failed`), and the mirror pre-doctor block using `--platform posix`; (8) both installers SKIP the check with a clear log line (`[installer] SHA256SUMS cross-check skipped (...)`) when the binaries dir is unset, preserving the dev-loop install-from-source path until Step 125's `verify-install` CI job makes an artefact directory mandatory - never silently pass through an existing but empty dir (unset check is by variable presence, not directory contents). Tests: new `BE/tests/app/test_installer_checksums.py` (14 cases): parser accepts text + binary mode, missing sums -> `E_INSTALL_MANIFEST_MISSING`, malformed line reports lineno, duplicate filename refused, `verify_release_binaries` happy-path windows + posix (parametrised over `BINARIES` so a new entry auto-covers itself), missing binary -> `binary-file-missing` reason, mismatch -> `E_CLI_CHECKSUM_MISMATCH` with `Actual != Expected`, inventory-missing-from-sums -> `inventory-missing-from-sums` reason, plus 5 CLI numeric-exit contract cases (0 / 10 / 11 / 12 / 13) each grepping the `[NN]` marker and, for 10 + 13, the wire code. Verified: `python3 -m pytest BE/tests/app/test_installer_checksums.py -q` -> **14 passed**. Not fixed (deliberate scope): no end-to-end `verify-install` CI job (Step 125); the two installers still allow the skip path (removed in Step 125 once CI proves the always-on flow); no Authenticode signing overlay (Step 141) so a valid signed binary is still just a `Signed=false` row in `install.json`; no PATH junction / uninstall path work (Steps 126-130). Version bump: v4.63.0 -> v4.64.0.

## v4.63.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 123: **Release workflow scaffold `.github/workflows/release.yml` with windows-latest + ubuntu-latest matrix, PyInstaller onefile builds, and per-platform SHA256SUMS.txt.** Root cause guarded (one sentence): Steps 117-122 landed the PyInstaller specs, `install.json` `Binaries` schema, `record-binary` CLI, and the Pester exit-code gate, but nothing on the release path actually built or published the onefile exes, so every downstream consumer (SHA256 cross-check in `install.ps1`, `verify-install` CI job, signing job, release page) was validating synthetic artefacts only. Minimum correct fix: (1) new `.github/workflows/release.yml` with `workflow_dispatch` trigger (tag trigger `on: push: tags: [v*]` is documented but commented out until Step 125's `verify-install` gate lands so we can never publish an unverified release); (2) `concurrency: group: release-${{ github.ref }} cancel-in-progress: true` so a rapid re-dispatch cannot race two builds writing the same artefact name; (3) `build` job with `fail-fast: false` matrix (`windows-latest` -> `.exe` outputs, `ubuntu-latest` -> POSIX ELF outputs, both using the SAME `.spec` files from Step 117 so the frozen entry surface is identical); (4) `PYTHONHASHSEED=0` + `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` + pinned `pyinstaller==6.11.1` keep SHA256 stable across re-runs on the same host (paired with the Step-117 `strip=False`, `upx=False` spec invariants); (5) `Emit BINARIES inventory` step reads `BE.app.installer_binaries.BINARIES` as JSON so the build loop and the SHA256 loop iterate the same source of truth (a new `BinaryEntry` added to the frozen tuple is picked up here with zero workflow edits); (6) `Build each onefile spec` iterates that JSON and runs `pyinstaller <spec> --clean --noconfirm --distpath dist --workpath build` (--clean forces a fresh build/ dir so a cached .pyc from a prior matrix leg cannot leak into another arch); (7) `Compute SHA256SUMS.txt` emits `<hex><space><space><filename>` per binary in GNU coreutils format so `sha256sum -c` on Linux and PowerShell `Get-FileHash` comparison in Step-124's `install.ps1` both parse it, and hard-fails when a build artefact is missing (no silent skips); (8) `actions/upload-artifact@v4` with `if-no-files-found: error` and 14-day retention per platform; (9) `publish` job gated on `publish_draft == 'true' AND release_tag != ''` (two-input gate makes accidental publication a syntax error) uses `softprops/action-gh-release@v2` to cut a _draft_ release with `prerelease` auto-flagged on `-rc` / `-beta` tag suffixes, flattens per-platform artefact bundles under `release/` prefixed with the platform, emits one authoritative combined `SHA256SUMS.txt`, and requires `permissions: contents: write`; (10) release body documents that binaries are NOT Authenticode-signed (signing lands in Step 141) so consumers know `install.json` rows will carry `Signed=false`. Tests: new `BE/tests/app/test_release_workflow_scaffold.py` (5 cases) - workflow file exists, declares matrix + concurrency + two-input publish gate, every `BINARIES` `SpecPath` resolves on disk (parametrized so a new entry auto-covers itself), and the workflow imports `BE.app.installer_binaries` (contract that the inventory-driven loop is preserved). Verified: `pytest BE/tests/app/test_release_workflow_scaffold.py -q` -> **5 passed**; YAML parses cleanly under `pyyaml`, matrix and triggers reflect the intent (`jobs: ['build','publish']`, `triggers: ['workflow_dispatch']`, matrix = `[windows-latest .exe, ubuntu-latest '']`); inventory JSON round-trip produces the expected two-row payload (`db-bootstrap`, `retention-run`); Pester harness unchanged and green at **11 passed** from Step 122. ## v4.62.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 122: **Extend the Pester harness to cover `Register-RetentionTask.ps1` 9520 / 9521 / 9524.** Root cause guarded (one sentence): Step 120 only exercised `Invoke-DbBootstrap.ps1` and `Invoke-RetentionRun.ps1`, so `Register-RetentionTask.ps1` exits 9520 (schtasks.exe missing) and 9524 (neither pwsh.exe nor powershell.exe on PATH) could silently regress on any `Common.psm1` or `Resolve-PwshExe` edit and nothing in CI would catch it. Minimum correct fix: (1) `Invoke-Wrapper` gains `-ExtraArgs [string[]]` and `-PathOverride [string]` so a call site can pass `-Install` to the wrapper and rewrite `$env:PATH` around the child `pwsh` spawn (restored in `finally` so parallel tests do not leak env state); (2) new helper `New-FakeSchtasks -FakeRepo` materialises an executable `schtasks.exe` shim (POSIX gets `chmod +x`) in `<FakeRepo>/fakebin/` so `Get-Command schtasks.exe` resolves on POSIX pwsh and `Assert-Schtasks` passes without needing the real Windows binary (the stub is never actually invoked; `Render-Xml` exits at 9521 / 9524 first); (3) new `Describe 'Register-RetentionTask.ps1 exit-code contract'` block with three `It` cases: `9520` (PathOverride=''), `9521` (template intentionally absent while venv-python + `Invoke-RetentionRun.ps1` are populated so template-missing is the only trigger), and `9524` (all three Render-Xml preflight artifacts present so the flow reaches `Resolve-VisionAppPwshExe`, which returns `$null` because PATH contains only the fake schtasks dir); (4) each case asserts both `ExitCode = Expected-Exit -Full <code>` (POSIX 8-bit truncation aware: 9520 -> 48, 9521 -> 49, 9524 -> 52) and a stderr grep for the `[NNNN]` marker plus the human-readable substring, so the assertion survives POSIX exit-code truncation. 9522 (renderer rejected knobs) and 9523 (schtasks /Create failed) are deliberately deferred to the signed-runner release workflow: 9522 needs a working `BE.app` import graph inside the venv python, 9523 needs a schtasks stub that returns non-zero AND a writable `%TEMP%` shape; both land with Step 121 (release workflow scaffold). Verified: `nix run nixpkgs#powershell -- -NoProfile -Command "Invoke-Pester -Path tests/pester/Wrappers.Tests.ps1 -Output Detailed"` -> **11 passed, 0 failed** (was 8; +3 Register cases). No BE code changed, so `pytest BE/ -q` unchanged at 1256 passed / 2 skipped. Not fixed (deliberate scope): 9522 / 9523 coverage; Windows-only exit-code full 32-bit assertions (CI still runs on ubuntu-latest until Step 121 wires a `windows-latest` matrix leg); no PSScriptAnalyzer rule added specifically for `Register-RetentionTask.ps1` (the existing Step-116 job already covers it). Version bump: v4.61.0 -> v4.62.0.

## v4.61.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 120: **Pester harness for `scripts/ps/` wrapper exit-code contract + `pester-wrappers` CI job.** Root cause guarded (one sentence): Step 116 wired PSScriptAnalyzer so wrapper _syntax_ is gated, but nothing ran the wrappers to prove `9510` / `9511` / `9530` / `9531` still fire on missing-venv and missing-target-script, so a helper refactor in `Common.psm1` could silently break the exit-code contract that `packaging/installers/install.ps1` and `Register-RetentionTask.ps1` depend on. Minimum correct fix: (1) new `tests/pester/Wrappers.Tests.ps1` (Pester 5/6-compatible; helpers live in a `BeforeAll` on `$script:` scope because Pester isolates each `Describe`); (2) `New-FakeRepo` materialises `<tempRoot>/scripts/ps/{Common.psm1,<wrapper>.ps1}` so `Get-VisionAppRepoRoot`'s two-parent walk resolves back to the temp root, giving each case an isolated repo where `.venv/` and `bin/` can be selectively populated; (3) `Invoke-Wrapper` spawns a child `pwsh -NoProfile -NonInteractive -File <wrapper>` and captures `$LASTEXITCODE` + redirected stderr, so the wrapper's `[Console]::Error.WriteLine` markers stay observable even when POSIX truncates the numeric exit; (4) an `Expected-Exit` helper honours POSIX 8-bit exit-code truncation (`9510 -> 38`, `9511 -> 39`, `9530 -> 58`, `9531 -> 59`) on non-Windows while pinning the full 32-bit code on Windows, and every assertion also grep-matches the `[NNNN]` stderr marker which is truncation-immune; (5) `Common.psm1` gets four sanity assertions (all exports present, `Get-VisionAppRepoRoot` returns the real checkout, `Get-VisionAppVenvPython` returns the OS-appropriate path shape, helper is pure so a nonexistent `-RepoRoot` still returns a path); (6) new CI job `pester-wrappers` in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` installs `Pester >= 5.0.0` on ubuntu-latest (pwsh preinstalled) and runs `Invoke-Pester -Path tests/pester -Output Detailed -PassThru` with `$r.FailedCount -gt 0` failing the job. Verified: `nix run nixpkgs#powershell -- -NoProfile -Command "Invoke-Pester -Path tests/pester/Wrappers.Tests.ps1 -Output Detailed"` -> **8 passed, 0 failed** (4 Common.psm1 + 2 Invoke-DbBootstrap + 2 Invoke-RetentionRun). Not fixed (deliberate scope): `Register-RetentionTask.ps1` 9520-9524 codes are not covered (needs a fake `schtasks.exe` stub on `PATH`; deferred to Step 121 where the release workflow builds a Windows-only stub); Windows-only exit-code assertions are not run because the sandbox is POSIX and GitHub-hosted `windows-latest` isn't wired here yet (the `Expected-Exit` helper leaves the door open, but the CI job runs on `ubuntu-latest` for now); no PSScriptAnalyzer regression check against `tests/pester/` (excluded because Pester `Should -Be` and `BeforeAll` idioms trip several stylistic rules). ## v4.60.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 119: **`bin/install-record.py record-binary` subcommand + backward-compat argv shim.** Root cause guarded (one sentence): before Step 119 the only writer of `install.json` was the flat action-append path, so `manifest.Binaries` stayed empty forever and the Step-118 `BinaryChecksumMismatch` doctor check was structurally unreachable in production. Minimum correct fix: (1) refactor `bin/install-record.py` from a flat argparse into subparsers `record-action` (unchanged Step-106 behaviour: `--install-root`, `--app-version`, `--platform`, `--name`, `--script`, `--args-json`, `--phase`, `--started-at`, `--completed-at`, `--duration-ms`, `--exit-code`, `--is-critical`, plus `--max-actions` / `--archive-max-bytes` for rotation) and `record-binary` (new: `--install-root`, `--app-version`, `--platform`, `--name` (inventory Name from `BE/app/installer_binaries.py`), `--exe-name`, `--exe-path`, `--signed {true,false}`, optional `--cert-thumbprint` and `--timestamped-at`); (2) add a compatibility shim in `_parse_args` that detects pre-Step-119 flat invocations (first argv token starts with `--`) and prepends `record-action`, so `packaging/installers/install.ps1` and `install.sh` keep working with no coordinated rev; (3) `record-binary` calls `BE.app.installer_signing.compute_binary_signature` (streams SHA256 of the exe at `--exe-path`) then `BE.app.install_manifest.record_binary` (upserts by `Name` so a re-record replaces the prior row); (4) validation happens before disk touch: `--signed true` without `--cert-thumbprint` -> exit `2` with `cert-thumbprint is required when --signed=true` on stderr; missing `--exe-path` file -> new exit code `4` (distinct from `2`=validation and `3`=manifest unwritable so the release workflow can distinguish a build-artefact drop from a signing bug); (5) on success emit one JSON stdout line `{Name, Sha256, SizeBytes, Signed}` (deliberately not envelope-wrapped: the release workflow greps this to cross-check `SHA256SUMS.txt` in Step 121). Tests: 7 new cases in `BE/tests/app/test_install_record_binary_cli.py` pin the flat-flags shim (`compat-shim` action lands in `manifest.Actions` from an argv that never mentions `record-action`), happy-path (`Sha256` matches `hashlib.sha256(exe.read_bytes()).hexdigest()`, stdout JSON matches the persisted row), upsert-by-name (two `record-binary` calls with different body bytes leave exactly one row with the second SHA256), missing-exe -> exit `4` and no manifest file created, `--signed true` without cert -> exit `2` with `cert-thumbprint` in stderr, signed+cert+timestamp persistence (`CertThumbprint`, `TimestampedAt` land verbatim), explicit `record-action` subcommand routing. Verified: `pytest BE/tests/app/test_install_record_binary_cli.py BE/tests/app/test_installer_doctor_and_record.py BE/tests/app/test_install_log_rotator.py -q` -> **all green**; full BE suite `pytest BE/ -q` -> **1256 passed, 2 skipped** (was 1249, +7). Not fixed (deliberate scope): `packaging/installers/install.{ps1,sh}` do not yet call `record-binary` (they only fingerprint action rows; the wrappers rev when the release workflow lands in Step 121+ so both learn the same call-site at once); no Authenticode signing itself (out of band on the signed Windows runner - this CLI only records the result an external `signtool` produced); no PyInstaller build here (sandbox has no PyInstaller so the CLI is validated against a synthetic exe body, same test pattern as Step 118). ## v4.59.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 118: **`install.json` schema v2 - `Binaries` field + `BE/app/installer_signing.py` + doctor tamper cross-check.** Root cause guarded (one sentence): Step 117 defined the `BINARIES` inventory but neither `install.json` nor any doctor path fingerprinted the produced `.exe`, so a tampered or replaced binary on disk (swap, missing Authenticode signature, size mismatch) was invisible to consumers and every uninstall assumed the on-disk file was still the one the release published. Minimum correct fix: (1) new `ManifestBinaryRecord` frozen dataclass persists one row per shipped exe (`Name`, `ExeName`, `Path`, `Sha256`, `SizeBytes`, `Signed`, `CertThumbprint`, `TimestampedAt`, `RecordedAt`); `InstallManifest` gains a `Binaries: list[dict]` field; (2) `MANIFEST_SCHEMA_VERSION` bumped to `2` with `_SUPPORTED_SCHEMA_VERSIONS = {1, 2}` so `read_manifest` accepts both and `write_manifest` normalises to v2 on the next write (v1 → v2 forward migration adds an empty `Binaries` list without a silent rewrite); (3) new pure module `BE/app/installer_signing.py` exposes `sha256_of_file(path) -> (hex, size)` (streaming 1 MiB reads, raises `E_INSTALL_MANIFEST_MISSING` on absent file, `E_INSTALL_MANIFEST_UNWRITABLE` on OSError) and `compute_binary_signature(*, name, exe_name, exe_path, signed=False, cert_thumbprint=None, timestamped_at=None, now=None)` returning a validated `ManifestBinaryRecord` with UTC ISO-8601 `RecordedAt` (seconds precision, matching the rest of the manifest); (4) new `record_binary(install_root, binary, *, app_version, platform, now)` upserts by `Name` (a re-install replaces the prior row so `install.json` always reflects the current exe) and `latest_binary(manifest, name)` mirrors `latest_action`; (5) `_validate_binary` enforces 64-char lowercase-hex `Sha256`, `SizeBytes >= 0`, `Signed=True` requires a `CertThumbprint`; (6) `run_doctor` now re-hashes every `manifest.Binaries` row at its recorded `Path` and emits `BinaryFileMissing` ERROR (recorded path absent, uninstall cannot verify what to remove) or `BinaryChecksumMismatch` ERROR (digest or size drift; Context includes both recorded and actual values) so a swapped/corrupted exe blocks the next install; the tamper check swallows I/O exceptions to keep the doctor crash-free but never swallows a mismatch. Tests: 12 new cases in `BE/tests/app/test_installer_signing.py` pin the v2 bump, v1 read + v2 write upgrade (json inspection confirms the on-disk SchemaVersion goes from 1 → 2), streaming SHA256 correctness against `hashlib.sha256`, missing-file error code, signature-record shape (Path normalises `\` → `/`), upsert-by-name behaviour (one row per Name after two records), `Signed`/`CertThumbprint` pairing rejection, `BinaryChecksumMismatch` severity + Context, `BinaryFileMissing` on exe deletion, and clean doctor when the file matches. Two Step-105 tests updated for the new constants (`SchemaVersion == MANIFEST_SCHEMA_VERSION`, invalid-schema now uses `99`). Verified: `pytest BE/tests/app/test_installer_signing.py BE/tests/app/test_install_manifest.py -q` -> **all green** (12 new + 21 existing); full BE suite `pytest BE/ -q` -> **1249 passed, 2 skipped** (was 1238, +11). Not fixed (deliberate scope): no `bin/install-record.py` CLI flag to invoke `compute_binary_signature` on real dist/ output (arrives with the release-workflow work in Step 121+); no Authenticode signing itself (out of band on the signed Windows runner - this module only _records_ the result); no `SHA256SUMS.txt` cross-check against manifest (Step 121 wires the release-workflow-generated file into the installer); no Pester harness (Steps 119-120); PyInstaller is not installed in the sandbox so the recorded SHA256 cannot be compared against a real onefile build here (validated end-to-end with a synthetic exe body in the test suite instead). Version bump: v4.58.0 -> v4.59.0.

Plan 90 Step 117: **PyInstaller onefile specs for `bin/db-bootstrap.py` and `bin/retention-run.py` + `BE/app/installer_binaries.py` inventory + doctor enforcement.** Root cause guarded (one sentence): the CLI fleet shipped as loose `.py` files, so a Windows operator without a curated Python + venv hit an opaque wrapper `9530=venv-missing` exit with no fallback binary and nothing on the release path failed fast when a required spec file was missing. Minimum correct fix: (1) two new PyInstaller specs under `packaging/pyinstaller/` (`db-bootstrap.spec`, `retention-run.spec`) freeze each CLI into a deterministic onefile console `.exe` with `upx=False` (stable SHA256 across builds, required by `SHA256SUMS.txt`), `console=True` (Universal Envelope on stdout, human progress on stderr), `strip=False`, stable output names (`db-bootstrap`, `retention-run`) so wrappers do not carry version-suffix collisions, and `datas=[("BE/db/migrations","BE/db/migrations")]` on the bootstrap spec so the frozen exe does not require a checkout on the target host; (2) new pure module `BE/app/installer_binaries.py` exposes a frozen `BINARIES` tuple (Name, SpecPath, EntryScript, ExeName, Purpose) and a `binary_presence(repo_root)` helper that only touches `Path.is_file` (mirrors the `installer_wrappers.py` pattern from Steps 115/116); (3) `run_doctor(..., repo_root=repo_root)` now populates `DoctorReport.Binaries` alongside `Wrappers` and emits two new ERROR findings when a spec or its entry script is missing (`BinarySpecMissing`, `BinaryEntryMissing`); (4) `DoctorReport.to_dict()` includes the `Binaries` list; (5) `render_human` prints `[binary spec:ok|MISSING entry:ok|MISSING] Name -> ExeName` lines per entry so operators see spec+entry presence before the finding list. Tests: 8 new cases in `BE/tests/app/test_installer_binaries.py` pin inventory shape (`db-bootstrap`, `retention-run`), frozen-dataclass immutability, real-checkout presence sanity (`test_all_specs_and_entries_exist_in_real_checkout` fails immediately if a spec or entry is deleted), empty-root all-False, doctor emits one `BinarySpecMissing` + one `BinaryEntryMissing` per entry with ERROR severity, real-repo doctor emits neither, `to_dict` payload includes `Binaries` with both `SpecPresent` and `EntryPresent`, backward compat when `repo_root` is omitted (no Binaries, no Binary\* findings), and spec-file smoke (entry-script name appears in each spec, `upx=False`, `console=True`). Verified: `pytest BE/tests/app/test_installer_binaries.py BE/tests/app/test_installer_wrappers.py BE/tests/app/test_installer_doctor_and_record.py BE/tests/app/test_ps_wrappers_common_import.py -q` -> **36 passed**; full BE suite `pytest BE/ -q` -> **1238 passed, 2 skipped** (was 1229, +9). Minimum correct fix: rename the two Windows-only template placeholders (`PYTHON_EXE` -> `PWSH_EXE`, `RETENTION_SCRIPT` -> `WRAPPER_SCRIPT`), rewrite `<Arguments>` to `-NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "${WRAPPER_SCRIPT}" --loop --interval-hours ${INTERVAL_HOURS} --retention-days ${RETENTION_DAYS}` (quoted so `Program Files` paths survive), rename the two matching kwargs on `render_windows_task_xml(...)` in `BE/app/retention_installer.py`, add a `Resolve-PwshExe` helper to `Register-RetentionTask.ps1` that prefers `pwsh.exe` and falls back to `powershell.exe` (new wrapper-only exit `9524 = neither pwsh.exe nor powershell.exe found`, printed via `[Console]::Error.WriteLine` so `$ErrorActionPreference='Stop'` cannot swallow it), point `$Wrapper = Join-Path $RepoRoot 'scripts/ps/Invoke-RetentionRun.ps1'` (dropping the old `$Retention = ... bin/retention-run.py`), extend the `Test-Path` preflight to reject a missing wrapper with the existing 9521 slot, and pass `pwsh_exe=$PwshExe` + `wrapper_script=$Wrapper` into the here-string Python renderer call. Zero changes to `bin/retention-run.py`, `BE/app/retention_audit.py`, `Invoke-RetentionRun.ps1`, or any envelope surface. XML injection guard (spec/21-app/79 §Validation-rules) still bars `"`, `<`, `>`, `\x00` on the two Windows kwargs, so the hardcoded quotes around `${WRAPPER_SCRIPT}` in the template cannot be smuggled open by a caller-supplied path. Spec 79 signature line updated to `render_windows_task_xml(*, pwsh_exe, wrapper_script, ...)` and the validation bullet widened to cover both `python_exe`/`retention_script` (systemd path) and `pwsh_exe`/`wrapper_script` (Windows path). Tests (`BE/tests/app/test_retention_installer.py`): 4 Windows cases updated to the new kwarg names, `Command.text` now asserts `pwsh.exe`, `Arguments.text` asserts `-NoProfile`, `-ExecutionPolicy Bypass`, `-File`, `Invoke-RetentionRun.ps1`, `--loop`, `--interval-hours 12`, `--retention-days 30`; injection + range + no-unresolved-placeholder cases carried over unchanged. Verified: `pytest BE/tests/app/test_retention_installer.py -q` -> **25 passed**. Not fixed (deliberate scope): no PSScriptAnalyzer run in CI (Step 116); no PyInstaller onefile (Step 117); no `install.ps1` manifest entry for the wrapper (Step 118); no Pester harness (Steps 119-120); no runtime execution of `schtasks /Create /XML` in the sandbox (schtasks + pwsh both absent, xml.etree parseability is the substitute proof per spec 79 §Acceptance-2).
