## v4.30.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 89: **Tolerance resolver** (`BE/app/rules/kernel/tolerance.py`) implements spec 21-app/34 end-to-end and wires into the rule kernel. New public surface: `ToleranceKind` enum (`ScalarRange | PercentRange | XyBox | MatchPercent`), frozen dataclasses `ToleranceProfile` (`profile_id`, `profile_name`, `kind`, validated `params`) and `ResolvedTolerance` (`primary`, optional `secondary`, `source_layer`), and two entrypoints: `build_profile_map(profiles_json, task_id) -> dict[str, ToleranceProfile]` and `resolve_for_rule(rule, profiles, task_id) -> ResolvedTolerance`. Validation covers spec 34 §3 per kind: `ScalarRange` requires numeric `Min <= Max` (bool rejected because Python bool is int subclass); `PercentRange` requires `0 <= MinPercent <= MaxPercent <= 100`; `MatchPercent` requires `0 <= MinPercent <= 100`; `XyBox` requires integer `CenterX/Y` and `HalfWidthPx/HalfHeightPx >= 1`; every kind that carries `Inclusive` validates it against `{Both, MinOnly, MaxOnly, Neither}` (spec 34 §3.1). Bundle-scope uniqueness enforced: duplicate `ProfileId` or `ProfileName` within the same Task raises `ToleranceInvalid`. Spec 34 §4 compat matrix pinned as `_ALLOWED` (`PresenceAbsence`->`MatchPercent`, `FlawDetect`->`ScalarRange`, `Count`->`ScalarRange` + optional `MatchPercent` secondary, `OcrText`->`MatchPercent`, `GraphicDisplayCheck`->`MatchPercent`, `MathExpression`->`ScalarRange`); `XyBox` binds to Regions (§67), so any rule-kind `ToleranceRef` naming an `XyBox` profile is `ToleranceIncompatible` on both Primary and Secondary slots. Spec 34 §5 resolution: `resolve_for_rule` reads `ToleranceRef` (required) and `SecondaryToleranceRef` (optional per spec 36 §6); missing / null / empty `ToleranceRef` -> `ToleranceUnresolved`; unknown id -> `ToleranceUnresolved` with `Slot=Primary|Secondary`; optional `TaskId:profileId` scoped ref is accepted when the prefix equals `task_id` and rejected as `ToleranceCrossTask` otherwise; `SecondaryToleranceRef` may be null but not empty-string. Spec 34 §7 error taxonomy: three sub-cases (`ToleranceInvalid`, `ToleranceCrossTask`, `ToleranceUnresolved`) all ride on the new wire code `E_TOLERANCE_UNRESOLVED` and carry the distinguishing string in `Context.ReasonCode`; the fourth (`ToleranceIncompatible`) rides on the new wire code `E_TOLERANCE_INCOMPATIBLE`, matching the `DomainError` vs `BugError` split in spec 40 §A.7. Both new codes register in `BE/errors/codes.py` and map to HTTP 422 (bundle-load unprocessable, consistent with `E_RULE_BUNDLE_INVALID`). Engine hook (`BE/app/rules/kernel/engine.py`): new `_preflight_tolerances(ctx, bundle)` runs before the evaluation loop and is opt-in - only activates when `ctx.metadata["ToleranceProfiles"]` is present, keeping every existing kernel test (Steps 79-88) unchanged. When active, it resolves every non-Inactive rule using `task_id = ctx.metadata.get("TaskId") or bundle.bundle_id`; failures matching `_TOLERANCE_FAILURE_CODES = {E_TOLERANCE_UNRESOLVED, E_TOLERANCE_INCOMPATIBLE}` become effective-Inactive via a SKIPPED judgment whose `details` carry `RuleStatus=Inactive`, `StatusReason=<ReasonCode>`, and `ErrorCode=<wire code>` (spec 24 §3 lets FE enumerate them; spec 34 §7 forbids silent pass). Any other AppError from the resolver is re-raised - loud failure only. New `_effective_counts` recomputes `active|silent|inactive` post-inactivation so the RuleSet counters (spec 24 §3) stay consistent. The main loop injects both `PriorJudgments` (Step 88) and `ResolvedTolerances: dict[str, ResolvedTolerance]` into a cloned `RuleContext.metadata`, giving evaluators the typed window without re-parsing bundle metadata (the surface Step 96 will persist as `ResolvedToleranceSnapshotJson`). Inactivated rules do NOT trigger short-circuit stop (their upstream demotion is not an author-visible Fail), and the short-circuit post-processing loop now also skips inactivated rules so the SKIPPED-for-short-circuit judgment doesn't overwrite the SKIPPED-for-tolerance judgment. Root cause of the pre-Step-89 gap (one sentence): the kernel had no place to enforce spec 34's threshold contract, so authored rules could ship with `ToleranceRef` values that reference missing / cross-task / incompatible-kind profiles and the kernel would happily evaluate them using whichever literal thresholds the evaluator's `params` still carried, silently passing anything the resolver should have blocked. Tests: `BE/tests/app/rules/test_tolerance.py` (34 cases): profile validation for all four kinds including bool-rejected-as-non-numeric, out-of-range percent bounds, inverted ranges, unknown kind, missing/duplicate ProfileId, duplicate ProfileName across distinct ids, XyBox zero half-extent, XyBox float center; positive resolution for `PresenceAbsence + MatchPercent`, `Count + ScalarRange primary + MatchPercent secondary`; task-scoped ref accepted for owning task; negative resolution for missing `ToleranceRef`, unknown ref, incompatible kind (both slots), cross-task ref, unknown rule kind; engine deactivation for unresolved and incompatible refs (asserts `verdict=SKIPPED`, `StatusReason`, `ErrorCode`, and inactive counter increment while active counter decrements); opt-out when `ToleranceProfiles` absent (Inactive rule with empty params passes through unchanged, preserving Steps 79-88 test contracts); metadata injection of `ResolvedTolerances` via a probe evaluator swapped into `predicates._REGISTRY` (asserts the ResolvedTolerance instance and both metadata keys land); non-triggering of short-circuit stop when a preceding rule is deactivated (asserts subsequent Active rule still evaluates and `stopped_early=False`). Verified: `python -m pytest BE/tests/app/rules/ -q` -> `207 passed`; `python -m pytest BE/ -q` -> `850 passed, 2 skipped` (was `816 passed, 2 skipped`; delta = +34 new tolerance cases). Unblocks Step 90 (bundle loader validates cross-rule DAG using resolved tolerances), Step 91 (per-rule timeouts read from the resolved tolerance kind), Step 96 (TaskDB `RuleResult` writer persists `ResolvedToleranceSnapshotJson`). Version bump: v4.29.0 -> v4.30.0.

Plan 90 Step 88: `MathExpression` predicate (spec 33 §3.6) lands as the sixth rule-kind evaluator, replacing the Step 81 stub and closing the v1 matrix (only `OcrText` remains disabled-in-v1 per spec 33 §4). New module `BE/app/rules/evaluators/math_expression.py` (`evaluate_math_expression`, registered via the `BE/app/rules/evaluators/__init__.py` import side-effect). Engine change (`BE/app/rules/kernel/engine.py`): `evaluate_bundle` now builds a per-run `prior: dict[str, RuleJudgment]` and clones `ctx` via `dataclasses.replace(ctx, metadata={**ctx.metadata, "PriorJudgments": prior})` before the rule loop; after each judgment lands, `prior[rule.id] = judgment`. Caller-owned metadata is untouched (immutability preserved). This makes the spec 33 §6 forward-ref ban structural: a rule that references a peer at or after its own orderIndex simply won't find it in the dict at evaluation time and surfaces as `RuleBadInput` (no runtime reordering, no forward-ref magic - matches "workers must reject" language in spec §6). Safe-arithmetic evaluator: parses the authored `Expression` string with `ast.parse(mode="eval")` and walks a strict allowlist. Zero use of `eval`, zero name lookups outside the four-func whitelist, zero `builtins` reach-in - which pins the safe-expression surface BEFORE Step 89 tolerance resolver ships (that step reuses the same evaluator; anything less strict would let author bundles smuggle `__import__("os").system(...)` through a tolerance formula). Allowed AST nodes: `Expression`, `Constant` (int/float only; `True`/`False` rejected because Python bool is int subclass and would silently pass as 0/1), `UnaryOp(USub)`, `BinOp(Add|Sub|Mult|Div)`, `Call(Name in {min, max, abs, round})`, `Attribute` chain in exactly the form `Rule.<id>.<key>`. Rejected as `RuleBadInput` with echoed `Expression`: `**` (Pow), `//` (FloorDiv), `%` (Mod), bitwise, compare, boolop, ternary (IfExp), lambda, comprehensions, subscripts, string literals, bool literals, bare `Name` outside whitelist, attribute chains other than `Rule.*`, keyword args, wrong-arity (`abs()`, `min(1)`, `round(1, 2)`), divide-by-zero (echoed as `ReasonDetail=DivideByZero`), non-finite result (NaN/Inf). Reference resolution: `Rule.r1.MatchCount` -> `ctx.metadata["PriorJudgments"]["r1"].details["MatchCount"]`. Missing `PriorJudgments`, missing rule, missing output key, non-numeric output, or referenced rule with verdict `Error`/`Skipped` -> `RuleBadInput` with `RefRuleId`, `OutputKey`, and (where applicable) `RefVerdict` or `OutputType` echoed for FE overlay. Outputs (`RuleJudgment.details`): `Value` (float rounded 6dp), `ResolvedInputsJson` (list of `{Ref, Value}` in first-seen evaluation order, deduplicated by Ref - so `Rule.b.V + Rule.a.V + Rule.b.V` reports `b` first then `a` once each), plus echoed `Expression`, `MinValue`, `MaxValue`, `RuleId`, `RuleKind`. Verdict: Pass when `MinValue <= Value <= MaxValue` (inclusive on both sides - verified at boundary), else Fail with `ReasonCode=RuleOutOfRange` (spec 33 §4 explicit for `Count` / `MathExpression`). Root cause of the prior gap (one sentence): the Step 81 stub raised `RuleUnsupported` for `MathExpression`, so any authored bundle with a math rule would drop to Error - which meant Step 89 (tolerance resolver reads the same safe-arithmetic surface), Step 90 (loader validates cross-rule DAG), and Step 96 (TaskDB persists Value/ResolvedInputsJson) had no reference implementation of the ref-resolution + safe-expression contract to build against; freezing it now is what unblocks those three steps in parallel. Registry housekeeping: `BE/tests/app/rules/test_predicates.py` `test_other_stubs_report_unsupported` is retired (no non-Ocr stubs remain) and replaced with `test_no_kind_remains_unsupported_stub` (asserts every non-Ocr kind's registered predicate is in the `rule_kernel.evaluators` module namespace - catches future stub regressions); `test_stub_error_becomes_error_judgment_not_exception` switched to `OcrText` (still `RuleDisabledInV1`) to keep the AppError->Error-judgment conversion covered. Tests: `BE/tests/app/rules/test_math_expression.py` = 44 cases including registration-replaces-stub, literal expression Pass, ref Pass and out-of-range Fail, whitelisted func combo (`max/min/abs/round`) end-to-end, boundary inclusive at 0 and 10, ResolvedInputs dedup order (`b, a, b` -> `[b, a]`), missing/empty/non-string/whitespace Expression parametrized, missing/non-numeric/bool/None/inverted bounds parametrized, 20-case parametrized forbidden-expression sweep (Pow, FloorDiv, Mod, bitwise, compare, `__import__`, `os.system`, bare name, list, generator, lambda, wrong arity x3, kwargs, `not`, ternary, string concat, `True + 1`), divide-by-zero, syntax error, missing rule ref, missing output key, ref-to-errored-rule, non-numeric output, missing prior context, end-to-end engine integration with a Count-substitute upstream, engine forward-ref (`r1` references `r2` which comes after) yields `RuleBadInput` on `r1`. Verified: `python -m pytest BE/ -q` -> `816 passed, 2 skipped` (was `772 passed, 2 skipped`; delta = 44 new + 0 net from stub-test edits). Unblocks Step 89 (tolerance resolver reuses `_evaluate_expression` for `Rule.<id>.<key>` interpolation into thresholds), Step 90 (bundle loader now has all six kinds' output contracts to validate the rule DAG end-to-end), Step 96 (TaskDB `RuleResult` writer persists `Value` + `ResolvedInputsJson` shape). Plan 90 Step 87: `GraphicDisplayCheck` predicate (spec 33 §3.5) lands as the fourth real evaluator, replacing the Step 81 stub. New module `BE/app/rules/evaluators/graphic_display_check.py` (`evaluate_graphic_display_check`, registered via the `BE/app/rules/evaluators/__init__.py` import side-effect). Refactor: NCC core extracted from `BE/app/rules/evaluators/count.py` into a shared `BE/app/rules/kernel/template.py` (public `luma`, `ncc_score_map`, `nms_peaks`) so `Count` and `GraphicDisplayCheck` share one implementation, killing the copy-paste risk that would have surfaced when `MathExpression`-adjacent tolerance work (Step 88+) touches template matching. `Count` now imports the shared helpers; behaviour + test surface unchanged (23 Count tests still pass). Contract pinned by this step: (1) required params `MinMatchPercent` (numeric, 0..100), `AllowRotationDeg` (non-empty list, values restricted to the closed set `{0, 90, 180, 270}`; empty list, non-list, or any out-of-set value including negatives, 360, string forms, or bool - `True`/`False` are int in Python and must be explicitly rejected - raises `RuleBadInput`), `AllowMirror` (strict bool; string "yes" or int 1 rejected). This pins the loader validation contract for spec 33 §3.5 rotation semantics BEFORE Step 90 loader ships. (2) Pattern injection reuses the Step 86 `ctx.metadata["Patterns"][rule.id]` contract (ndarray, ndim matches ROI, spatial dims positive; oversized handled by orbit-fit check below rather than up-front bail). (3) Search space: outer loop `mirror_options = (False, True) if AllowMirror else (False,)`, inner loop over unique `AllowRotationDeg` values; for each `(mirror, deg)` variant compute `np.fliplr` then `np.rot90(k=deg//90)` on the luma pattern; skip variants whose spatial dims exceed the ROI (a rotated 10x8 pattern becomes 8x10 at 90/270 degrees, which is legitimate authoring). (4) If NO variant fits the ROI -> `AppError(E_RULE_EVAL_FAILED, ReasonCode=RuleBadInput)` with `RoiShape`, `PatShape`, `AllowRotationDeg` echoed. This is critical: silent Pass on a rule that can't be evaluated is exactly the anti-pattern spec 40 forbids. (5) Winner selection via `local_max > best_score` (strict >), first fit is kept on ties so the deterministic tiebreak is `(mirror=False, first-rotation-listed)`. (6) Outputs: `MatchPercent` = best score in [0, 100] rounded 4dp, `AppliedRotationDeg`, `AppliedMirror`. (7) Verdict: Pass when `MatchPercent >= MinMatchPercent`, else Fail with `ReasonCode=RuleBelowThreshold`; message echoes `BestRot` and `Mirror` for FE overlay. (8) Details echo `MinMatchPercent`, `AllowRotationDeg`, `AllowMirror`, `RuleId`, `RuleKind`. Root cause of the prior gap (one sentence): Step 86's `_ncc_score_map` was private to `count.py`, so `GraphicDisplayCheck` would have either duplicated it (drift risk) or reached into a private symbol (fragile import); extracting to `template.py` now pins the shared surface before three more callers accumulate. Regression: `test_predicates.py` cases that assumed `GraphicDisplayCheck` was a stub switched to `MathExpression` (still a stub until Step 88), preserving engine `AppError`->Error-judgment coverage. Tests: `BE/tests/app/rules/test_graphic_display_check.py` = 19 cases covering registration-replaces-stub, zero-rotation exact match (`AppliedRotationDeg==0`, `AppliedMirror==False`), 90-degree rotation selected when placed frame contains the rotated variant (`AppliedRotationDeg==90`), mirror selected when placed frame contains fliplr variant (`AppliedMirror==True`; pattern intentionally asymmetric on the horizontal axis - left column brightened - so the mirror is distinguishable from the original), rotation-not-allowed Fail (`RuleBelowThreshold`), bad-input taxonomy (missing MinMatchPercent/AllowRotationDeg/AllowMirror parametrized, invalid rotation values `[45, -90, 360, "0", None]` parametrized, empty rotation list, non-bool mirror, no-variant-fits-ROI, missing patterns dict, end-to-end Pass and Error via `evaluate_bundle`). Verified: `python -m pytest BE/ -q` -> `772 passed, 2 skipped` (was `753 passed, 2 skipped`; delta = 19 new + 0 net from stub switch). Unblocks Step 88 (`MathExpression`: last of six rule kinds, no template reuse but shares the same `RuleBadInput` bad-input helper pattern), Step 89 (tolerance resolver reads outputs from all six kinds), Step 90 (bundle loader validates every non-Ocr kind end-to-end - all four template-based evaluators now agree on the pattern-injection contract), Step 96 (TaskDB `RuleResult` writer persists all six kinds' details shapes). Version bump: v4.27.0 -> v4.28.0.

## v4.27.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 86: `Count` predicate (spec 33 §3.3) lands as the third real evaluator, replacing the Step 81 stub. New module `BE/app/rules/evaluators/count.py` (`evaluate_count`, registered via the `BE/app/rules/evaluators/__init__.py` import side-effect). Contract pinned by this step: (1) required params `MinCount` (int >= 0), `MaxCount` (int, must be >= `MinCount`; ordering violation is `RuleBadInput`), `MinMatchPercent` (numeric, 0..100), plus `SearchRegion.XyBox` from the Step 82 ROI reader. (2) Pattern injected via `ctx.metadata["Patterns"][rule.id]` and MUST be a numpy ndarray whose `ndim` matches the ROI (2D or 3D) and whose spatial dims fit inside the sliced ROI; missing dict, missing per-rule entry, non-ndarray, ndim mismatch, non-positive dims, or oversized pattern all raise `AppError(E_RULE_EVAL_FAILED, ReasonCode=RuleBadInput)` with `PatShape`/`RoiShape` echoed - matches the Step 85 "no synthetic pixels" invariant and pins the pattern-injection contract needed by Step 87 (`GraphicDisplayCheck` reuses the helper) and Step 90 (loader can validate every non-Ocr kind end-to-end). (3) Algorithm: vectorized normalized cross-correlation on unweighted luma via `numpy.lib.stride_tricks.sliding_window_view`; `win_dev = window - window_mean`, `pat_dev = pattern - pattern_mean`, score = `sum(win_dev * pat_dev) / (||win_dev|| * ||pat_dev||)`; zero-variance tiles (uniform window or uniform pattern) score 0 to avoid divide-by-zero false positives; negative correlation clipped to 0 so "anti-matches" never count. (4) Non-max suppression: greedy `argmax` over the score map, zero out a pattern-sized neighbourhood around each peak, repeat until score falls below `MinMatchPercent/100` or 512 peaks retained (spec cap). (5) Match centroid math: `X = roi_local_top_left_x + SearchRegion.X + pattern_width // 2`, same for Y, so `MatchesJson` is in frame coordinates matching FlawDetect's coordinate convention. (6) `MatchesJson` sorted by `MatchPercent` desc, each entry `{X, Y, MatchPercent}`. (7) Verdict per spec 33 §3.3: Pass when `MinCount <= MatchCount <= MaxCount`, else Fail with `ReasonCode=RuleAboveThreshold` (over cap) or `ReasonCode=RuleBelowThreshold` (under floor), message echoes counts for FE overlay. (8) Details echo `MinCount`, `MaxCount`, `MinMatchPercent`, `RuleId`, `RuleKind` alongside outputs. Regression: `test_predicates.py` cases that assumed `Count` was still a stub switched to `GraphicDisplayCheck` (which stays a stub until Step 87), preserving engine `AppError`->Error-judgment coverage. Tests: `BE/tests/app/rules/test_count.py` = 23 cases covering registration replaces-stub, single/multi-match Pass paths, frame-space centroid offset by SearchRegion origin + pattern half-extent, match sort order, above-max Fail (`RuleAboveThreshold`), below-min Fail (`RuleBelowThreshold`), zero-count boundary, NMS collapsing overlapping high-score windows to a small count, bad-input taxonomy (missing MinCount/MaxCount/MinMatchPercent parametrized, MaxCount<MinCount, MinMatchPercent out-of-range, missing Patterns dict, missing per-rule pattern, pattern larger than ROI, ndim mismatch 2D vs 3D, non-ndarray pattern), 512-cap on `MatchesJson`, end-to-end Pass via `evaluate_bundle`, end-to-end Error when pattern absent (engine `AppError`->Error, never silent Pass). Root cause of the prior gap (one sentence): Steps 82/85 landed the frame + reference contracts but no evaluator consumed a per-rule template ndarray, so Step 90 (loader) had no `Patterns` shape to validate and Step 87 (`GraphicDisplayCheck` reuses template matching) had no reusable NCC helper, forcing template-loading semantics to be invented twice or drift. Verified: `python -m pytest BE/ -q` -> `753 passed, 2 skipped` (was `730 passed, 2 skipped`; delta = 23 new + 0 net from stub switch = +23). Unblocks Step 87 (`GraphicDisplayCheck`: reuses `_ncc_score_map` + rotation/mirror wrapper), Step 90 (bundle loader: pattern-injection contract now pinned - `ctx.metadata["Patterns"][rule.id]` is authoritative), Step 96 (TaskDB `RuleResult` writer can persist real `MatchesJson` shape), Step 131+ (FE authoring form for Count rule can render `MinCount`/`MaxCount`/`MinMatchPercent` against a concrete schema). Version bump: v4.26.0 -> v4.27.0.

## v4.26.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 85: `FlawDetect` predicate (spec 33 §3.2) lands as the second real evaluator, replacing the Step 81 stub. New module `BE/app/rules/evaluators/flaw_detect.py` (`evaluate_flaw_detect`, registered against `predicates.register("FlawDetect", ...)` at import time via the `BE/app/rules/evaluators/__init__.py` side-effect). Contract pinned by this step: (1) required params `Sensitivity` (numeric, 0..100), `MinFlawAreaPx` (int >= 0), `MaxAllowedFlawCount` (int >= 0), plus `SearchRegion.XyBox` from the Step 82 ROI reader; optional `MaskRegions` = list of `{XyBox: {X, Y, W, H}}` in frame coords, intersected with the ROI and zeroed out of the deviation mask before labelling (mask outside the ROI is a no-op, not an error, matching authoring UX). (2) Reference frame injected via `ctx.metadata["References"][rule.id]` and MUST be a numpy ndarray with the same shape as the sliced ROI (2D or 3D matching the frame); missing dict, missing per-rule entry, non-ndarray, or shape mismatch all raise `AppError(E_RULE_EVAL_FAILED, ReasonCode=RuleBadInput)` with `RefShape`/`RoiShape` echoed in details, never fabricated - matches Step 82's "no synthetic pixels" invariant and pins the reference-injection contract needed by Step 90 (loader) and Step 156+ (Daheng vendor adapter). (3) Sensitivity -> per-pixel luma threshold via `max(1, round(255 * (1 - S/100)))` so `Sensitivity=100` still requires a non-zero delta (identical pixels never count as flaws, otherwise every 8-bit noise pixel would). (4) Luma uses unweighted channel mean for 3-channel frames (color-order-agnostic, matching PresenceAbsence). (5) Connected components implemented as pure-numpy 4-connectivity iterative flood fill (`_label_components`); NO scipy dependency added so `BE/pyproject.toml` stays clean per Step 82's dependency discipline. (6) Component stats vectorized via `np.bincount(weights=)` for centroid sums; centroids offset by `SearchRegion.X/Y` so `FlawCentroidsJson` is in frame coordinates (spec 33 §3.2 requirement), sorted by `AreaPx` desc, capped at 128 entries (spec cap), and each entry is `{X, Y, AreaPx}`. (7) `MinFlawAreaPx` drops sub-threshold components from the retained set BEFORE the `MaxAllowedFlawCount` comparison, matching spec 33 §3.2 verdict predicate ("Pass when `FlawCount <= MaxAllowedFlawCount` and every flaw >= `MinFlawAreaPx`"). (8) Verdict: Pass when retained `FlawCount <= MaxAllowedFlawCount`, else Fail with `ReasonCode=RuleAboveThreshold` and a message that echoes count/largest/sensitivity for FE overlay. (9) Details echo `Sensitivity`, `MinFlawAreaPx`, `MaxAllowedFlawCount`, `RuleId`, `RuleKind` alongside the outputs so the FE can render reasoning without a second lookup, exactly mirroring PresenceAbsence's shape. Root cause of the prior gap (one sentence): Step 82 landed the reference-free `PresenceAbsence` and Steps 83-84 pinned telemetry + short-circuit contracts, but no evaluator yet consumed `ctx.metadata["References"]`, so Step 90 (bundle loader) and Step 156+ (vendor adapter) had no concrete reference-injection contract to code against, and Step 96 (TaskDB persistence) would have shipped without a real defect-shaped `FlawCentroidsJson` sample forcing schema drift later. Tests: `BE/tests/app/rules/test_flaw_detect.py` = 20 cases covering registration replaces-stub, identical frames -> Pass with zero flaws, single flaw fails when over threshold with correct centroid, flaw within allowance passes, `MinFlawAreaPx` filter drops tiny flaws, centroids sorted desc by area, centroids are frame coords offset by box origin, MaskRegion suppresses flaw inside its intersection with ROI, bad-input taxonomy (missing Sensitivity/MinFlawAreaPx/MaxAllowedFlawCount parametrized, Sensitivity out-of-range parametrized, missing References dict, missing per-rule reference, reference shape mismatch with dims echoed, MaskRegions non-list, MaskRegions missing XyBox), end-to-end Pass via `evaluate_bundle`, end-to-end Error when reference absent (engine converts `AppError` to Error judgment - never silent Pass), and centroid cap at 128 with unfiltered `FlawCount` still reported. Regression: `test_predicates.py` cases that assumed `FlawDetect` was still a stub switched to `Count` (which stays a stub until Step 86), preserving the engine's `AppError`->Error-judgment coverage without weakening it. Verified: `python -m pytest BE/ -q` -> `730 passed, 2 skipped` (was `709 passed, 2 skipped`; delta = 20 FlawDetect + 1 net from Count stub switch = +21). Unblocks Step 86 (`Count` predicate: reuses `_luma`, ROI reader, and pattern-matching skeleton), Step 90 (bundle loader: reference-injection contract now pinned - `ctx.metadata["References"][rule.id]` is authoritative), Step 96 (TaskDB `RuleResult` writer can persist a real `FlawCentroidsJson` payload against a real-shape sample), Step 156+ (Daheng vendor adapter knows exactly what reference shape to load at bundle-prep time). Version bump: v4.25.0 -> v4.26.0.

Plan 90 Step 84: Short-circuit checkpoint telemetry. `BE/app/rules/kernel/models.py::Verdict` gains `SKIPPED = "Skipped"`; `RuleResult` gains four fields (`skipped_count: int`, `stopped_early: bool`, `stop_reason: str | None ("FirstFail" | "FirstError")`, `stop_at_rule_id: str | None`, `total_latency_ms: float`). `BE/app/rules/kernel/engine.py::evaluate_bundle` refactored: loop is now index-based; on the first Active FAIL or ERROR under `mode="short-circuit"` it records `stop_reason`+`stop_at_rule_id`+`stop_index` and breaks, then a second pass over `bundle.rules[stop_index+1:]` emits `_skipped_judgment(rule)` for every non-inactive remainder (SILENT rules included, INACTIVE still excluded). Skipped judgments carry `Verdict.SKIPPED`, `ReasonCode=ShortCircuitStop`, `RuleId`, `RuleKind`, `RuleStatus`; message `"skipped: short-circuit stop upstream"`. Counter invariant preserved: skipped judgments do NOT contribute to `pass_count`/`fail_count`/`error_count`, so `pass+fail+error <= active` holds. `total_latency_ms` measured around the entire loop via `telemetry.perf_now_ms` (kernel purity intact: predicates still receive no clock). Root cause of the prior gap (one sentence): Step 79's short-circuit path silently dropped remaining rules from the judgments tuple, so a `mode=short-circuit` run and a `mode=full` run with fewer rules were indistinguishable on the wire, blocking Step 96 persistence from faithfully replaying a session and Step 131+ FE from rendering "these rules never ran because rule X failed first". Tests: `BE/tests/app/rules/test_short_circuit.py` = 6 cases pinning first-Fail-stops-and-marks-rest-skipped (ids preserved, verdicts sequenced PASS/FAIL/SKIPPED/SKIPPED, `stop_reason="FirstFail"`, `skipped_count=2`, counter invariant), first-Error-flags-`FirstError`, `mode=full` never short-circuits (`stopped_early=False`, `skipped_count=0`), Silent-after-stop gets Skipped marker with `RuleStatus="Silent"` echoed, Inactive-after-stop stays excluded from judgments (`"r2" not in ids`), and `total_latency_ms >= 0`. Verified: `python -m pytest BE/ -q` -> `709 passed, 2 skipped` (was 703; +6 short-circuit tests). Unblocks Step 85 (FlawDetect can trust the stop contract when authored inside a short-circuit bundle), Step 96 (TaskDB `RuleResult.Skipped` / `StoppedEarly` / `StopReason` columns bind to real data), Step 131+ (FE renders greyed-out "skipped due to short-circuit" chips with the culprit rule id). Version bump: v4.24.0 -> v4.25.0.

## v4.24.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 83: Per-rule telemetry lands on every `RuleJudgment.details`. New module `BE/app/rules/kernel/telemetry.py` exposes `perf_now_ms()` (monotonic wall-time via `time.perf_counter`), `roi_hash(sub)` (SHA-256 over dtype + shape + contiguous bytes so different dtypes with identical byte layout do NOT alias), `try_roi_hash(ctx, rule)` (best-effort wrapper that returns `None` when the frame or `SearchRegion` is absent/invalid - those cases already surface as an Error judgment via the predicate, so a missing hash is NOT silent failure), `predicate_version(fn)` (reads `__predicate_version__` attribute with `KERNEL_PREDICATE_VERSION="1"` fallback). `BE/app/rules/kernel/engine.py` refactored: `_evaluate_one` now wraps `_dispatch` with `perf_now_ms` before/after, injects `LatencyMs` (float, ms, rounded to 4dp), `PredicateVersion` (string), and `RoiHash` (hex string, omitted when None) into the judgment via `dataclasses.replace`. Telemetry lands on ALL three verdicts (Pass, Fail, Error) so downstream persistence (Step 96) and release audit (Step 111+) have a uniform schema regardless of outcome. Predicate lookup failure (unregistered kind) also carries telemetry via the extracted `_error_judgment` helper. Root cause of the prior gap (one sentence): Step 82 lands real evaluators but every judgment omitted observability, so operators cannot answer "which rules are slow", "did the ROI change between runs", "which predicate build produced this Fail" - without telemetry captured at the kernel boundary, Step 96 TaskDB writer would have to synthesize placeholders and Step 111+ release automation would ship without a version-audit trail. Injecting at `_evaluate_one` (not per-predicate) prevents drift as more kinds land in Steps 85-88. Kernel purity preserved: `perf_now_ms` is called ONLY from the engine's telemetry wrapper, never from a predicate, so evaluators remain clock-free and unit-testable with pinned `evaluated_at`. Tests: `BE/tests/app/rules/test_telemetry.py` = 6 cases pinning Pass-carries-full-telemetry (LatencyMs float >= 0, PredicateVersion="1", RoiHash 64-hex), Error-still-carries-latency-and-version (missing Frame -> Error verdict, LatencyMs present, RoiHash OMITTED not zeroed), unknown-kind gets kernel default version, RoiHash stability across identical inputs + content-sensitivity, dtype-distinction (uint8 zeros vs int8 zeros -> different hashes), and `__predicate_version__` attribute override wins over default. Verified: `python -m pytest BE/ -q` -> `703 passed, 2 skipped` (was `697 passed, 2 skipped`; delta = 6 telemetry tests). Unblocks Step 84 (short-circuit path can now log per-checkpoint latency), Step 96 (TaskDB `RuleResult.LatencyMs`, `RoiHash`, `PredicateVersion` columns bind to real data), Step 111+ (release audit trail has a build-id column per judgment). ## v4.23.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 82: ROI reader + first real predicate land. New modules: `BE/app/rules/kernel/roi.py` (`slice_search_region(ctx, rule) -> np.ndarray`), `BE/app/rules/evaluators/__init__.py` (side-effect registration), `BE/app/rules/evaluators/presence_absence.py` (`evaluate_presence_absence`, registered against `predicates.register("PresenceAbsence", ...)`). Root cause of the prior gap (one sentence): Step 81 registered stubs for every closed `RuleKind` but zero real evaluators, so no kind evaluated end-to-end (frame in -> `RuleJudgment` out) and Steps 96+ persistence, Step 131+ FE bundle-preview, and the Step 156+ vendor adapter would have shipped against synthetic judgments then re-drifted the moment a real evaluator landed. Contract pinned by this step: (1) ROI reader is pure - reads `RuleContext.metadata["Frame"]` (numpy `ndarray`, `.ndim in {2,3}`) and `params.SearchRegion.XyBox = {X, Y, W, H}`, returns a numpy VIEW (not a copy) so downstream evaluators can slice further without allocating - matching the Step 79 purity rule; (2) every failure raises `AppError(E_RULE_EVAL_FAILED, ReasonCode=RuleBadInput)` with `RuleId`/`RuleKind`/`RunId` context plus the specific offender (`Got` type, `Ndim`, `X`/`Y`/`W`/`H`, `FrameH`/`FrameW`), never returns `None`, never uses a try/except to hide the input error; (3) PresenceAbsence follows spec 33 §3.1 verdict matrix exactly (Pass when `Mode=Present AND MatchPercent >= MinMatchPercent`, Pass when `Mode=Absent AND MatchPercent < MinMatchPercent`, else Fail with `ReasonCode=RuleBelowThreshold`); (4) outputs surface all spec-required keys (`MatchPercent`, `MatchedX`, `MatchedY`, `MatchedShapeKind`) plus echoed `Mode`/`MinMatchPercent`/`IntensityThreshold` so the FE overlay can render the reasoning without a second lookup; (5) `MatchPercent` is the placeholder-real coverage proxy: pixel-count above `IntensityThreshold` (default 32, range [0,255]) divided by ROI size - flagged as placeholder in the module docstring, replaced when Step 85's FlawDetect and Step 86's Count pattern-matching land; (6) centroid `(MatchedX, MatchedY)` is offset by `XyBox.X/Y` so coordinates are in frame space, not ROI space (matches spec 33 §3.1 output semantics); (7) 3-channel frames compute luma as unweighted mean across channels (color-order-agnostic, since the kernel makes no BGR-vs-RGB assumption); (8) evaluator input validation is loud: bad `Mode`/`MinMatchPercent`/`IntensityThreshold` all raise `RuleBadInput`, engine converts to Error judgment (never silent Pass); (9) registration is side-effecting at import time - `import rule_kernel.evaluators` once activates the real dispatch table, matching Step 81's registry contract. Tests: `BE/tests/app/rules/test_roi.py` = 11 cases (missing Frame, non-ndarray, bad ndim, missing SearchRegion, missing XyBox, non-numeric fields, non-positive W/H, negative X/Y, out-of-bounds with frame dims echoed, view-mutation contract, 3-channel shape); `BE/tests/app/rules/test_presence_absence.py` = 15 cases pinning stub-replaced-by-real registration, full 2x2 verdict matrix (Present/Absent x pass/fail), centroid offset by box origin, 3-channel luma path, bad-mode parametrize (4 values), missing MinMatchPercent, out-of-range MinMatchPercent parametrize (3), bad IntensityThreshold parametrize (3), and end-to-end through `evaluate_bundle` for both Pass (`pass_count=1`, `MatchPercent=100.0`) and Error (missing Frame -> `RuleBadInput` -> Error judgment). ## v4.22.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 81: Predicate registry lands as `BE/app/rules/kernel/predicates.py` and is wired through `BE/app/rules/kernel/engine.py::evaluate_bundle`. Root cause of the prior gap (one sentence): Step 79's `evaluate_bundle` was a no-op skeleton that always returned Pass with zero judgments and zero counter increments, so without a single dispatch contract every downstream evaluator (Step 82 ROI reader, Step 83 telemetry, Step 84 short-circuit path, Steps 131+ FE authoring, Steps 156+ vendor adapters) would have invented its own kind->callable wiring and drifted on the reason-code taxonomy. Contract pinned by this step: (1) `Predicate = Callable[[RuleContext, RuleSpec], RuleJudgment]` is pure - no I/O, no clock, no vendor calls - matching the kernel purity rule from Step 79; (2) every closed `RuleKind` from `spec/21-app/33-rule-catalog.md` §3 (`PresenceAbsence`, `FlawDetect`, `Count`, `OcrText`, `GraphicDisplayCheck`, `MathExpression`) is registered with a `NotImplemented` stub raising `AppError(E_RULE_EVAL_FAILED)` carrying the spec-defined reason code - `RuleDisabledInV1` for `OcrText` per spec 33 §4, `RuleUnsupported` for the rest - so unknown or unimplemented kinds NEVER silently pass; (3) `register(kind, fn)` refuses kinds outside the closed catalog (adding a new kind requires a spec 33 update, not a registration hack); (4) engine dispatch converts predicate `AppError` into an Error judgment, preserving `details.ReasonCode`/`RuleId`/`RuleKind` so operators see WHY a rule errored, not just that it did; (5) non-`AppError` exceptions escape (they indicate kernel bugs, not evaluator failures, and must not be silently converted); (6) Silent rules are evaluated + emitted as judgments but do NOT contribute to `pass_count`/`fail_count`/`error_count` or overall verdict per spec 33 §Rule Status; (7) Inactive rules are skipped entirely (predicate never called - proven by call-spy test); (8) overall verdict precedence Error > Fail > Pass matches spec 22 §4; (9) `mode="short-circuit"` early-exits Active-rule iteration at first FAIL or ERROR (Silent judgments after the exit point are also skipped, matching the spec 49 §4 stop semantics); (10) loader `_RULE_KINDS` and registry `_KNOWN_KINDS` are cross-checked by test so the two closed catalogs cannot drift silently. Tests: `BE/tests/app/rules/test_predicates.py` = 11 cases pinning registry-covers-catalog, loader/registry sync, `OcrText`->`RuleDisabledInV1`, other-stubs->`RuleUnsupported`, register-rejects-unknown, get-unknown-raises, stub-error-becomes-error-judgment (details.ReasonCode preserved), pass/fail roll-up (Active), silent-emitted-but-not-counted, inactive-never-called (call-spy), short-circuit stops at first Active FAIL, and Error > Fail precedence. Existing `test_kernel.py` counter-invariants test survives unchanged because its "noop" kind rules now flow through the stub-error path and still satisfy `pass+fail+error <= active`. Verified: `python -m pytest BE/tests/app/rules/ BE/tests/errors/test_new_codes.py BE/tests/cli/processing/test_verify_bundle.py -q` -> `65 passed`; full BE suite `python -m pytest BE/ -q` -> `666 passed, 2 skipped`. ## v4.21.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 80: Bundle loader lands as `BE/app/rules/kernel/loader.py`, exporting `load_bundle(path, *, strict_kind=False) -> RuleBundle`. Root cause of the prior gap (one sentence): Step 79 pinned the in-memory `RuleBundle` contract, but `BE/cli/processing/commands/evaluate.py` and `BE/cli/processing/commands/verify_bundle.py` each hand-parsed JSON with duplicated shape checks, so any future kernel consumer (watch loop, dry-run, verify, FE bundle-preview endpoint at 131+) would drift on rule format and Steps 81-95 predicates would have no single ingest to bind against. Loader mirrors the `verify_bundle` acceptance contract (closed `RuleKind` from spec 33 §3, closed `Status` from §3a, `acceptanceConditions` shape from spec 60), collects ALL problems into `details.Problems[]` before raising ONE `AppError(E_RULE_BUNDLE_INVALID)`, and never returns a partial bundle. `validationMode` maps per `spec/21-app/49-validation-order.md` §4 (`parallel`->`full`, `sequential`->`short-circuit`); missing value uses the safe `full` default, unknown value surfaces as `ValidationModeUnknown` problem AND defaults to `full` so the anomaly is visible in the envelope rather than silently coerced. `strict_kind` matches the existing `verify-bundle --strict-kind` flag (missing `kind` becomes a problem instead of forward-compat allowlist). `enabled: false` maps to `RuleStatus.INACTIVE` only when `status` is absent, preserving the explicit-wins precedence in `verify_bundle._classify_status`. ## v4.19.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 78: `GlobalErrorModal` gains deep-link chips into the observability UI. New component `src/components/errors/SessionLinks.tsx` is mounted at the bottom of the `OverviewTab` in `src/components/errors/GlobalErrorModal.tsx` (import added, JSX inserted after the existing `EnvelopeErrorPanel` block). Root cause of the prior gap (one sentence): Steps 75-77 shipped `/observability/sessions`, `/logs`, and `/ipc` viewers keyed on `CliInvocationId`, but any `E_CLI_*` / `E_IPC_*` wire error still surfaced as an opaque banner with no path back into those viewers, forcing operators to hand-copy the id from a toast into the URL bar. Implementation: `extractCliInvocationId(err)` reads `err.context?.CliInvocationId` first (canonical PascalCase used across BE routes) and falls back to `cli_invocation_id` for pre-envelope legacy paths; a non-integer or non-positive value renders nothing (never fabricate a link target, per `spec/03-error-manage/` loud-failure rule). When an id is present the component renders three `<Link>` chips plus a visible `CliInvocationId: N` chip so the operator can copy the id even if the routes are offline. Chips (in order): (1) Sessions -> `to="/observability/sessions"`; (2) Logs -> `to="/observability/sessions/$cliInvocationId/logs"` with `params={{ cliInvocationId: idStr }}`; (3) IPC -> `to="/observability/sessions/$cliInvocationId/ipc"` with the same params and optional `search={{ mailbox }}`. `extractMailbox(err)` validates `err.context?.Mailbox` (or lowercase `mailbox`) against the fixed BE Step 74 allowlist `{worker-out, processing-in, processing-out, main-in}`; unknown values collapse to `null` so BE's `CliInvocation.CliName`-derived default (`worker-cli`->`worker-out`, `processing-cli`->`processing-out`) wins. Code-prefix highlighting: `err.code` is uppercased once, and `E_CLI_*` gets a red-500 border + a red code badge on the Logs chip while `E_IPC_*` gets the same treatment on the IPC chip, so the operator's eye lands on the correct tier before reading. Every link uses `<Link to>` + `params` per `tanstack-navigation` rules (never `<a href>`), preserving preloading and type safety; the underscore-prefixed layout segment does not appear in the route strings because these routes are not under one. `title` attributes describe each destination so hover explains the target without requiring a click. Verified: `bunx tsgo --noEmit` -> 0 errors. Unblocks the rule-kernel arc (Steps 79-95) which will start throwing `E_RULE_*` codes: the same `SessionLinks` surface will absorb any new prefixes without a route rewrite, since the id-based deep-link contract is the invariant. Version bump: v4.18.0 -> v4.19.0.

Plan 90 Step 77: FE `IpcMonitor` lands as `src/routes/observability.sessions.$cliInvocationId.ipc.tsx` bound to `src/lib/observability/ipc.functions.ts`, a TanStack server-function proxy over BE `GET /observability/sessions/{cli_invocation_id}/ipc` (Step 74). Root cause of the prior gap (one sentence): Steps 75-76 exposed sessions and per-session logs, but the FrameReady/ResultReady/Heartbeat/Error traffic that crosses worker->processing->main as one-file-per-message drops under `<APP_IPC_ROOT>/<mailbox>/*.msg.json` was still invisible, forcing operators to shell into the IPC root and `cat` files by hand to diagnose IPC-tier bugs (missing acks, RunId leakage between sessions sharing a mailbox, poison messages). Implementation: server-fn keeps `BE_URL` off the browser bundle (defaults `http://127.0.0.1:8787`, overridable via `process.env.BE_URL`), Zod-validates input (`cliInvocationId` >=1, `mailbox` in allowlist `{worker-out, processing-in, processing-out, main-in}`, `limit` 1..500, `includeAcked` bool, `afterMsgId` cursor) and the Universal Envelope response, unwraps `Data`, and re-throws the first `Errors[]` entry as `"<Code>: <Message>"` so the UI banner shows the wire code (`E_BE_NOT_FOUND: mailbox directory missing on disk`, `E_BE_BAD_REQUEST: unknown mailbox`) instead of a plain HTTP status. Route params-driven (`/observability/sessions/{cliInvocationId}/ipc`); mailbox picker offers auto (BE derives from `CliInvocation.CliName`: `worker-cli`->`worker-out`, `processing-cli`->`processing-out`) plus all four allowlisted values so operators can peek at any mailbox for cross-tier debugging; cursor paging via `afterMsgId = NextAfterMsgId` so history is NEVER re-fetched; Load-more button is disabled unless the last page reported `IsTruncated=true` AND a `NextAfterMsgId` is present (prevents a phantom no-op click at the tail of a mailbox); `includeAcked` toggle mirrors the BE param so operators can see completed traffic alongside pending; `limit` is bounded client-side to 1..500 to match the server contract. Rendering: each item shows a kind badge (`FrameReady` sky, `ResultReady` emerald, `Error` red, `Heartbeat` muted, unknown fallback), `IsAcked` state chip (acked emerald / pending yellow), timestamp, `From -> To` routing arrow, optional `Seq`, `MsgId` on the right, and a collapsed JSON view of `Payload`. Poison-item contract mirrors BE Step 74: items shaped `{_ParseError, _Raw, IsAcked}` render as red-bordered rows with the parse error, the on-disk path, and ack state visible (loud-failure per `spec/03-error-manage/`, never silently dropped). Header surfaces `RunId`, `Mailbox`, absolute `MailboxPath`, and a `more available` badge when `IsTruncated=true`. Back link to `/observability/sessions`. Poll/paging errors surface into the same banner, never swallowed. `robots: noindex` (internal operator screen). Verified: `bunx tsgo --noEmit` -> 0 errors. Unblocks Step 78 (`GlobalErrorModal` now has deep-link targets for both `E_CLI_*` (into `/logs`) and `E_IPC_*` (into `/ipc?mailbox=<derived>`) codes) and closes the CLI observability UX arc anchored by Steps 75-77. Version bump: v4.17.0 -> v4.18.0.

Route params-driven (`/observability/sessions/{cliInvocationId}/logs`); first render tails last 500 lines (BE default 200 with explicit override); opt-in Follow polls every 2000ms using `afterOffset = NextOffset` so history is NEVER re-read across polls; Pause freezes the view without dropping accumulated lines; Reload replays from tail. Header shows `RunId`, absolute `LogPath`, current `NextOffset`, and a `truncated` badge when the BE window clipped bytes. Back link to `/observability/sessions`. Auto-scroll to bottom only while following so a paused operator can hold their reading position. Poll errors surface into the same banner (never swallowed) so a failing tail is visible immediately. `robots: noindex`. Verified: `bunx tsgo --noEmit` -> 0 errors. ## v4.15.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 74: `BE/routes/observability/ipc.py` lands the read-only `GET /observability/sessions/{cli_invocation_id}/ipc` endpoint, the mailbox tail companion to Step 73's log tail. Wired into `BE/main.py` alongside `observability_sessions_route` and `observability_logs_route`; `BE/routes/observability/__init__.py` docstring extended. Root cause of the prior gap (one sentence): Step 73 shows what the CLI _said_ (its JSONL log), but IPC traffic (`FrameReady`, `ResultReady`, `Heartbeat`, `Error`) crosses the worker->processing->main boundary as one-file-per-message drops under `<APP_IPC_ROOT>/<mailbox>/*.msg.json` per `spec/21-app/76-cli-log-and-ipc.md`, so operators could enumerate sessions and read logs but had zero visibility into which frames a session actually produced or consumed, forcing a shell into the IPC root to `cat` files by hand. Implementation: mailbox default is derived from `CliInvocation.CliName` (`worker-cli`->`worker-out`, `processing-cli`->`processing-out`) so the FE never has to guess; explicit `?mailbox=` is validated against a fixed allowlist `{worker-out, processing-in, processing-out, main-in}` (a rejected value returns `E_BE_BAD_REQUEST` with the allowlist in `Details.Allowed` so operators self-serve); `?limit=1..500` (default 100) is bounded server-side; `?include_acked=true` opts in to reading `*.msg.ack.json` alongside `*.msg.json` so the tail can show completed traffic too; `?after_msg_id=<cursor>` pages forward using `MsgId` (ULID-ish, lexically monotonic per `BE/cli/common/ipc.py::_ulid`) rather than byte offset because messages are file-per-record, not JSONL. Without a cursor the endpoint returns the last `limit` files (tail); with a cursor it returns the first `limit` files whose `MsgId` sorts strictly after it. Messages are filtered to `RunId == CliInvocation.RunId` so a mailbox shared across runs never leaks another session's traffic. `MailboxPath` is resolved and `relative_to(APP_IPC_ROOT)` is enforced defense-in-depth even though the allowlist already blocks traversal; failure raises `E_BE_BAD_REQUEST` with both paths in `Details`. Loud-failure surface: unknown `CliInvocationId` -> `E_BE_NOT_FOUND` (404); mailbox dir missing on disk -> `E_BE_NOT_FOUND` (404) with `Details.Hint = run bootstrap_ipc_dirs() or check APP_IPC_ROOT` (never a silent `Items=[]` that would hide "IPC not initialised"); bad `mailbox` / bad `limit` -> `E_BE_BAD_REQUEST` (400); Root-DB missing `CliInvocation` -> `E_BE_INTERNAL` (500) with `Details.Hint = python bin/db-bootstrap.py` (matches Steps 72/73). Poison-file contract: unreadable files, invalid JSON, or non-object top-level surface as `{"_Raw": "<path>", "_ParseError": "...", "IsAcked": <bool>}` inside `Items` (loud-failure per `spec/03-error-manage/`); poison items are exempt from `RunId` filtering so operators still see corruption instead of losing it to a silent skip. Every item is decorated with `IsAcked` (derived from `.msg.ack.json` suffix) and `Path` (absolute) so the FE can render ack state and deep-link to the on-disk file. Structured logs: `ipc_tailed` (info) with `count`, `mailbox`, `is_truncated`, `mode`; `ipc_path_escape` and `ipc_query_failed` at ERROR level with `CorrelationId` + `subject_id`. Reads only via `BE.db.connections.get_root_conn`; never touches Task or Rules tiers (pinned by `test_tier_isolation_uses_root_only` which greps the source file for `get_task_conn`/`get_rules_conn`). Wire keys PascalCase: `CliInvocationId`, `RunId`, `Mailbox`, `MailboxPath`, `Count`, `IsTruncated`, `NextAfterMsgId`, `Items`. Coverage: `BE/tests/routes/test_observability_ipc.py` = 14 cases (default worker-out for worker-cli, default processing-out for processing-cli, explicit mailbox override, RunId isolation, include*acked toggle both states, tail-limit slicing with `IsTruncated=true` + correct `NextAfterMsgId`, cursor forward paging, poison-file surfacing with `IsAcked=false`, unknown-session 404, bad-mailbox 400 (traversal attempt), bad-limit 400 at both boundaries, missing-mailbox 404 with `Hint`, missing-`CliInvocation`-table 500 with bootstrap hint, tier-isolation source-grep). Verified: `pytest BE/tests/routes/test_observability_ipc.py` -> `14 passed`; full backend suite `pytest BE/tests/` -> `640 passed, 2 skipped` (was 626 + 14 new). Unblocks Step 75 (FE `SessionList` binds to Step 72), Step 76 (FE `LogTailViewer` binds to Step 73), Step 77 (FE `IpcMonitor` binds to this route with polling + cursor), and Step 78 (`GlobalErrorModal` deep-links `E_IPC*\*`codes to`/sessions/{id}/ipc?mailbox=<derived>`).

## v4.14.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 90 Step 73: `BE/routes/observability/logs.py` + `BE/routes/observability/__init__.py` (unchanged wiring) land the read-only `GET /observability/sessions/{cli_invocation_id}/logs` endpoint that binds `CliInvocation.LogPath` (Root-DB migration `BE/db/migrations/root/0010_root_cli_invocations.sql`) to the FE log-tail viewer (Step 76). Wired into `BE/main.py` alongside the Step 72 sessions router. Root cause of the prior gap (one sentence): Step 72 exposed sessions but each row's `LogPath` was dead metadata (the FE could enumerate `worker-cli`/`processing-cli` runs but had zero way to read what happened inside them), so any `E_CLI_*` failure surfaced as "session X failed exit 42" with no context and forced operators to shell into the host and cat the JSONL by hand. Implementation: poll-based (NOT SSE) to keep the handler shape identical to Step 72 and to stay compatible with edge runtimes and the existing uvicorn dev script; supports both first-page tail (`?tail=1..2000`, default 200) and incremental resume (`?after_offset=<byte>`) so the FE viewer can long-poll for new lines without re-reading history; `_read_tail` computes `NextOffset` as the last complete-newline offset (not `size`) so a partial trailing line the CLI is still writing does NOT get re-emitted on the next poll and does NOT get half-parsed into a poison record; `_read_from_offset` mirrors the same "hold back partial trailing line" contract. Loud-failure surface: unknown `CliInvocationId` -> `E_BE_NOT_FOUND` (404); `LogPath IS NULL` on the row -> `E_BE_NOT_FOUND` (404) with `Hint = check CliInvocation.ExitCode for the crash reason` (CLI died before opening its log); log file missing on disk -> `E_BE_NOT_FOUND` (404) with the resolved path in `Details` (never a silent `Items=[]` that would look identical to "log is empty"); `LogPath` escapes `APP_LOG_ROOT` -> `E_BE_BAD_REQUEST` (400) (path-traversal defense: even though `LogPath` is authored by our own `BE/cli/common/logger.py`, a tampered Root-DB row must not turn this endpoint into arbitrary file read); bad `tail`/`after_offset` -> `E_BE_BAD_REQUEST` (400) with `Details.Received`; Root-DB `CliInvocation` table missing -> `E_BE_INTERNAL` (500) with `Details.Hint = python bin/db-bootstrap.py` (matches Step 72's contract). Poison-line contract: unparseable JSONL lines surface as `{"_Raw": "<line>", "_ParseError": "..."}` inside `Items` so a single bad record NEVER silently drops from the tail (loud-failure per `spec/03-error-manage/`). All handler paths log with `CorrelationId`, `operation`, `code`, `subject_id`; success path logs `logs_tailed` with `count`, `next_offset`, `is_truncated`, `mode`; path-escape logs `logs_path_escape` at ERROR level with both resolved paths; SQL failure logs `logs_query_failed` at ERROR with the sqlite message. Uses only the guarded Root-tier connection via `BE.db.connections.get_root_conn`; never queries Task or Rules tiers. Wire keys are PascalCase (`CliInvocationId`, `RunId`, `LogPath`, `NextOffset`, `IsTruncated`, `Items`). Coverage: `BE/tests/routes/test_observability_logs.py` = 16 cases (happy tail, default-tail returns-all-when-smaller, offset resume, offset-past-EOF empty, partial trailing line held back with `NextOffset < size`, poison line surfaces as `_Raw`+`_ParseError`, unknown id 404, null-LogPath 404 with hint, missing-file 404, path-escape 400, four bad-tail boundary values, negative-offset 400, missing-table 500 with hint). Verified: `pytest BE/tests/routes/test_observability_logs.py BE/tests/routes/test_observability_sessions.py` -> `29 passed`; full backend suite `pytest BE/tests/` -> `626 passed, 2 skipped` (was 610 + 16 new). Loud-failure log lines confirmed firing in captured output: `"message": "logs_path_escape"` and `"message": "logs_query_failed"` at ERROR. Unblocks Step 74 (IPC monitor route reuses `_read_tail`/`_read_from_offset` primitives against `<APP_IPC_ROOT>/<mailbox>/*.jsonl`), Step 76 (FE `LogTailViewer` binds to a stable poll contract), and Step 78 (`GlobalErrorModal` deep-links `E_CLI_*` codes to `/sessions/{id}/logs?tail=200`).

Two subprocess-based tests fork a fresh Python interpreter, import `BE.cli.processing.main` plus every module discovered under `BE.cli.processing.commands.*` via `pkgutil.iter_modules`, dump `sys.modules`, and assert that none of `BE.sdk_facade.camera`, `BE.cli.worker.camera_lease`, `BE.cli.worker.subcommands`, `BE.cli.worker.main`, or any `gxipy.*` / `daheng.*` vendor module leaked into the graph. Root cause guarded: if camera code leaks into `processing-cli`, PyInstaller bundles bloat and the CLI crashes on hosts without vendor DLLs; an in-process scan would false-pass because sibling suites (`test_e2e_watch.py`, facade suites) already load camera code into the shared interpreter. Subprocess `cwd` pinned to repo root via `Path(__file__).resolve().parents[4]` because `BE.` package resolution requires rootdir-relative imports (running from `BE/` breaks `import BE`). Verified: `pytest BE/tests/cli/processing/test_no_camera_import.py -v` → `2 passed`; full CLI suite `pytest BE/tests/cli/` → `397 passed, 2 skipped`. Unblocks Step 70 (mirror boundary scan for `worker-cli` — asserts processing modules never leak the other way) and Steps 111-130 (PyInstaller specs can trust the boundary and split bundles cleanly).

## v4.3.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 88 Step 14: `BE/logging_config.py` installs structured JSON logging so Step-13 handlers' `extra={CorrelationId, operation, code, subject_id}` payloads actually reach ops (default `StreamHandler` silently drops them). `JsonFormatter.format()` emits one JSON object per record: `ts` (ISO-8601 UTC), `level`, `logger`, `message`, then the four known context fields hoisted to top level (default `None` when absent so log-parsing tools can index without knowing the message text). `_merge_extras()` copies any additional `extra=` keys (e.g. `retry_attempt`, `retry_budget` required by `spec/coding-guidelines/python.md` error rule 4) verbatim, filtered against `_LOGRECORD_RESERVED` (stdlib internals). `_fold_exception()` folds `logger.exception(...)` output into `exc_type` / `exc_message` / `stack` so tracebacks survive JSON serialization. `configure_logging(level: LogLevel)` is idempotent: it removes any prior handler tagged `_be_json=True` before attaching a fresh `StreamHandler(sys.stdout)` with `JsonFormatter`, so multi-call boot paths (test fixtures, hot-reload) can't stack duplicate handlers. All helpers ≤15 lines per runtime rule 1. Tests in `BE/tests/test_logging_config.py` (7 cases) lock: base `ts/level/logger/message` shape, hoisting of all 4 known fields, default-`None` when absent, extra-field merge, exception folding with traceback text intact and `CorrelationId` preserved, single-handler invariant across 3 `configure_logging` calls (final level = last call's level), and end-to-end stdout JSON round-trip. Verified: `python3 -m pytest BE/tests/test_logging_config.py -q` → `7 passed`; full BE suite → `42 passed`. Unblocks Step 15 (`create_app` calls `configure_logging(settings.log_level)` once at startup), Step 23 (pytest asserts on structured fields), all future routes (silent failure is now structurally impossible when handlers/routes use `logger.*(..., extra=...)`).

Plan 88 Step 13: `BE/errors/handlers.py` registers three FastAPI exception handlers so no error path escapes the frozen `{ok,data,error}` envelope. `handler_app_error` maps `AppError` → registry-driven HTTP status via `exc.http_status` and dumps `exc.to_envelope()`. `handler_validation` maps pydantic `RequestValidationError` → `E_BE_BAD_REQUEST` (400 per Step-11 registry, not FastAPI's default 422) with `details.errors = exc.errors()`. `handler_unhandled` catches bare `Exception` → `E_BE_INTERNAL` (500), calls `logger.exception(...)` so tracebacks land in logs, and redacts the wire `message` to `"internal error"` when `settings.is_prod` (dev keeps `TypeName: repr` for debugging). All three propagate `X-Correlation-Id` via `_correlation_id(request)` (caller value or fresh UUID4 through `ensure_correlation_id`) and log once with `CorrelationId`, `operation` (`"METHOD path"`), `code`, and `subject_id` per `spec/coding-guidelines/python.md` error rule 3. `register_exception_handlers(app)` is the single entry point Step 15 `create_app` will call. Helper functions kept ≤15 lines per guideline runtime rule 1. Tests in `BE/tests/test_handlers.py` (6 cases) exercise via `TestClient`: envelope + status match for `AppError`, correlation-id passthrough, UUID mint when header absent, validation → 400 envelope with `E_BE_BAD_REQUEST`, unhandled → 500 with `E_BE_INTERNAL` + correlation-id in details, prod redaction (leaked `"kaboom"` absent from response), and single-record log emission with all 4 context fields. ## v4.1.0 - 2026-07-21

Plan 88 Step 12: `BE/errors/apperror.py` implements the typed `AppError` boundary exception. Carries `(code: ErrorCode, message: str, details: dict, cause: BaseException | None)`; `details` defaults to `{}`; when `cause` is supplied it is also set as `__cause__` so tracebacks retain the vendor origin (per `spec/coding-guidelines/python.md` rule 28: vendor exceptions surface as typed adapter errors at the facade). `http_status` property delegates to `default_http_status(code)` so Step 13 handlers derive the response status from the central registry (no per-route `HTTPException(status=...)` drift). `to_envelope()` returns the frozen failure envelope built via `BE.envelope.failure(code.value, message, details)` so handlers serialize with one call. `__repr__` includes code/message/details for structured logs. Tests in `BE/tests/test_apperror.py` (8 cases, all passing) lock: typed code carry, `Exception` subclass identity, default empty details, status derivation for 3 families (`E_BE_NOT_FOUND`→404, `E_SEC_UNAPPROVED_EGRESS`→403, `E_CAM_TIMEOUT`→504), envelope wire shape match, `cause` and `__cause__` preservation on both direct and `raise ... from` paths, and diagnostic `__repr__`. Verified: `python3 -m pytest BE/tests/test_apperror.py -q` → `8 passed`. Unblocks Step 13 (single FastAPI handler for `AppError`), Steps 15-19 (routes raise `AppError(code, msg, details)` instead of `HTTPException`), Steps 20-22 (SDK facade wraps vendor exceptions as `AppError(..., cause=vendor_exc)`).

Every member maps to an `HTTPStatus` via `_STATUS`; `default_http_status(code)` returns the canonical response status (`E_BE_NOT_FOUND`→404, `E_SEC_UNAPPROVED_EGRESS`→403, `E_CAM_TIMEOUT`→504, etc.); `is_registered(value)` gives lint/import validators a single source of truth so any stray `E_*` literal becomes `E_BUG_UNKNOWN_CODE` per `spec/21-app/40-error-manage.md` §5. ## v3.989.0 - 2026-07-21

Coding-guidelines CI enforcement. Added `format:check` (`prettier --check .`) and `guidelines:check` (`format:check` + `typecheck:strict` + `lint`) npm scripts, plus a "Prettier (format check on changed files)" step in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `frontend-checks` job that runs before `bun run lint`. The gate is forward-only: it prettier-checks only files changed vs the PR base (or `HEAD~1` on push), skipping generated/vendored paths (`routeTree.gen.ts`, lockfiles, `worker/calibration*`, `worker/fixtures`, `dist`, `.output`, `.vinxi`). Existing unformatted files do not fail CI, but any touched `.ts/.tsx/.js/.jsx/.mjs/.cjs/.json/.css/.md/.yml/.yaml` must pass Prettier. Typecheck (`tsgo` + authoritative `tsc`) and ESLint gates already blocked; formatting is now the third leg of the coding-guidelines gate cited in `.lovable/coding-guidelines/coding-guidelines.md`. No runtime code changed.

## v3.988.0 - 2026-07-21

Existing `00-index.md` remains a table of contents; the README is now the canonical URL to cite from downstream implementer PRs (Steps 6-15 BE scaffold, 30-55 FE typed client, 56-66 launcher + shell). No source diagrams modified; no runtime code changed.

## v3.985.0 - 2026-07-21

Moved the editor action cluster (Saved / Reset / Search / Save / Preview / Publish) off the app titlebar and onto the section-tabs row per `assets/issues/21-move-editor-actions-to-tabs-row.md` (screenshot `assets/ui/83-move-actions-to-tabs-row.png`). `SectionTopBar.tsx` now renders the tabs in a `flex-1` scrollport with a right-aligned `#section-topbar-actions-slot` portal target. `EditorTopBar.tsx` resolves that slot first and only falls back to `#titlebar-editor-slot` when the section bar isn't mounted (SSR / narrow layouts).
